<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> For every dimension <i>d</i> ≥ 3 and every spin $`S\in\{\frac12,1,\frac32,\ldots\}`$, we prove that the nearest-neighbor isotropic quantum Heisenberg ferromagnet has a translation-invariant, spontaneously magnetized equilibrium state at every sufficiently low positive temperature. The same state satisfies the KMS condition for the zero-field dynamics and has magnetization at least $`S/4`$. This resolves the low-temperature ordering problem in the spontaneous-magnetization formulation.

- **claim-main-theorem** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For every integer $d\geq3$ and every $S\in\{\frac12,1,\frac32,\ldots\}$, there is a finite $\beta_0(d,S)>0$ such that, for every finite $\beta\geq\beta_0(d,S)$, the dynamics associated with <a href="#eq:hamiltonian" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:hamiltonian">[eq:hamiltonian]</a> has a translation-invariant $\beta$-KMS state $\omega$ satisfying $$\omega(S_0^z)\ge S/4.$$
  - Explanation: For every dimension at least three and every allowed spin there is a finite threshold inverse temperature. Above it, the zero-field dynamics has a translation-invariant equilibrium state whose one-site spin expectation is bounded below by a fixed fraction of the spin.
- **claim-slot-realization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-slot-realization)): At each site $x$, the operators $\widehat S_x^a=\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}s_{(x,j)}^a$ restrict on $\operatorname{Sym}^{\ell}(\mathbb C^2)$ to the spin-$S$ matrices defined in Section <a href="#sec:model" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:model">1.1</a>. The orthogonal projection onto this subspace is $$\mathcal S_x=\frac1{\ell!} \sum_{\kappa\in\mathfrak S_\ell}\mathcal U_{\kappa,x}.$$ With $\mathcal S_\Lambda=\prod_{x\in\Lambda}\mathcal S_x$, define $$\widehat H_\Lambda =-\sum_{\{x,y\}\subset\Lambda:\,\|x-y\|_1=1} \sum_{a=x,y,z}\widehat S_x^a\widehat S_y^a, \qquad \widehat M_\Lambda=\sum_{i\in V_\Lambda}s_i^z.$$ Both operators commute with every within-site permutation and with $\mathcal S_\Lambda$. Their restrictions to $\operatorname{ran}\mathcal S_\Lambda$ are unitarily equivalent to $H_\Lambda$ and $M_\Lambda=\sum_{x\in\Lambda}S_x^z$, respectively. Moreover, $$\label{eq:slot-hamiltonian} \widehat H_\Lambda =-\frac12\sum_{\{i,j\}\in\mathcal E_\Lambda} \mathcal U_{(ij)} +\frac{q_\Lambda}{4}I.$$
  - Explanation: A spin-S site is realized as several spin-one-half slots restricted to their symmetric subspace. The exchange interaction then becomes a sum of slot transpositions plus a constant.
- **claim-spin-loops** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-spin-loops)): For every finite cube $\Lambda$, spin $S$, and $0<\beta<\infty$ as above, the partition function is $$\label{eq:loop-partition-function} Z_\Lambda(\beta):=\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}_{\mathcal H_\Lambda}e^{-\beta H_\Lambda} =e^{\beta q_\Lambda/4}\, \mathbb E_{\mathrm b}2^{c(\omega)}.$$ More generally, for every function $\Phi$ on the finite spectrum of $M_\Lambda$, $$\label{eq:spectral-loop-law} \frac{\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}_{\mathcal H_\Lambda} \bigl(e^{-\beta H_\Lambda}\Phi(M_\Lambda)\bigr)} {Z_\Lambda(\beta)} =\frac{\displaystyle\mathbb E_{\mathrm b} \sum_{\varepsilon\in\{-1,1\}^{\{\text{cycles of }\omega\}}} \Phi\!\left(\frac12\sum_C\varepsilon_C a_C\right)} {\displaystyle\mathbb E_{\mathrm b}2^{c(\omega)}}.$$ Thus the spectral law of $M_\Lambda$ is obtained by weighting the base picture by $2^{c(\omega)}$, then coloring each cycle independently up or down with equal probabilities, and summing the corresponding $+1/2$ or $-1/2$ over its slots at time $0$.
  - Explanation: The partition function and the spectral law of total magnetization equal expectations over random exchange pictures, with each cycle weighted by a factor from its two spin colors and colored independently.
- **claim-dense-pins** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-dense-pins)): Fix a finite cube $\Lambda$, $0<\beta<\infty$, and a finite deterministic pin set $F$. Let $(\mathcal C_n)_{n\ge1}$ be increasing finite sets of times, each containing $0$, whose union is dense in $\mathbb R/\beta\mathbb Z$, and put $$D_{0,n}=\partial\Lambda\times\{1,\ldots,\ell\}\times\mathcal C_n, \qquad D_n=F\cup D_{0,n},\qquad D=F\cup D_0.$$ For $\mathbb P_{\mathrm b}$-almost every picture there is a finite $n_*(\omega)$ such that, for all $n\ge n_*(\omega)$, $$w_{D_n}(\omega)=w_D(\omega),\qquad \mathcal B_{D_n}(\omega)=\mathcal B_D(\omega).$$ The laws $\mathbb P_{D_n}$ converge to $\mathbb P_D$ in total variation. More generally, if $\Phi_n\to\Phi$ almost surely under $\mathbb P_{\mathrm b}$ and $\sup_n\|\Phi_n\|\le A<\infty$, then $$\label{eq:dense-pin-expectations} \mathbb E_{D_n}\Phi_n\longrightarrow\mathbb E_D\Phi.$$
  - Explanation: Continuous pinning of the boundary is approximated by pinning at finitely many boundary times. Weights, missing-cycle sets and laws agree eventually or converge in total variation.
- **claim-factorial-domination** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-factorial)): For every finite pin set $D$, or such a set united with $D_0$, every integer $n\ge1$, and every nonnegative measurable function $F:X_\Lambda^n\to[0,\infty]$, $$\label{eq:factorial-domination} \mathbb E_D\sum_{(u_1,\ldots,u_n)\in(\omega_{\mathrm m})^n_{\ne}} F(u_1,\ldots,u_n) \le 2^n\int_{X_\Lambda^n}F(u_1,\ldots,u_n) \prod_{j=1}^n\mu(\,\mathrm du_j).$$ In particular, the estimate holds with the marks restricted to any specified time interval and with any specified chronological order.
  - Explanation: Under any pin law, the expected sum over ordered tuples of distinct exchange marks is dominated by a constant power times the plain intensity integral, because inserting a mark can at most double the pin weight.
- **claim-simultaneous-pin-test** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-pin-test)): For every finite volume, every $\beta>0$, every pin set $E$ that is finite or a finite set together with $D_0$, and every nonempty finite single-time set $T$ disjoint from $E$, the return fraction in <a href="#eq:pin-return-fraction" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:pin-return-fraction">[eq:pin-return-fraction]</a> satisfies $$\label{eq:simultaneous-pin-test} \mathbb P_E(T\subseteq\mathcal B_E)\le (2r)^{\|T\|}.$$
  - Explanation: The probability that all points of a single-time test set lie on cycles missing the existing pins is bounded by a power of twice the mean return fraction.
- **claim-stability-closure** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-pin-stability-closure)): For polynomials in a fixed finite set of variables the following operations preserve stability, with the possibility of producing the zero polynomial: coefficientwise limits with a common degree bound, specialization of a variable to a real number, and partial differentiation. Identifying two variables preserves stability. In particular, identifying two variables as $t$ and extracting the coefficient of $t$ preserves stability up to zero.
  - Explanation: Stability of polynomials is preserved by coefficientwise limits, real specialization, differentiation and identification of variables, possibly yielding the zero polynomial.
- **claim-partial-symmetrization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-pin-partial-symmetrization)): Let $F$ be a stable multiaffine polynomial, let $\tau$ exchange two of its variables, and let $0\le u\le1$. Then $(1-u)F+u\tau F$ is stable and multiaffine.
  - Explanation: Mixing a stable multiaffine polynomial with its swap of two variables, with a weight between zero and one, keeps it stable and multiaffine.
- **claim-exchange-symbol** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-pin-exchange-symbol)): For a permutation obtained by a finite product of independent Bernoulli transpositions and deterministic permutations on a finite set $V$, the polynomial $\mathbb E F_\pi$ is stable. Here each Bernoulli transposition uses an arbitrary probability in $[0,1]$. The same conclusion holds for any limit of these permutation laws.* *Every finite independent product of finite-time Poisson exchange permutations, uniform permutations on specified subsets of $V$, and deterministic permutations has such a limit representation.
  - Explanation: The averaged product symbol of a random permutation built from independent Bernoulli transpositions and deterministic permutations is stable, and so are limits of such laws, including Poisson exchanges and uniform permutations.
- **claim-pin-splicing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-pin-splicing)): Starting from <a href="#eq:pin-layered-symbol" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:pin-layered-symbol">[eq:pin-layered-symbol]</a>, eliminate each $q\in V\setminus P$ by setting $x_q=y_q=t$ and extracting the coefficient of $t$. The resulting nonzero stable polynomial is $$\label{eq:pin-spliced-symbol} F_P(\boldsymbol x,\boldsymbol y) =\mathbb E_{\mathrm b} \left[2^{n_P(\pi)}\prod_{i\in P}(x_i+y_{\sigma(i)})\right].$$ If $T\subseteq P$, then the probability generating polynomial of the random subset $\sigma(T)$ under $\mathbb P_P$ is stable. More precisely, with $Z_P=\mathbb E_{\mathrm b}2^{n_P(\pi)}>0$, it is $$\label{eq:pin-all-x-zero} G_T(\boldsymbol y) :=\mathbb E_P\prod_{i\in T}y_{\sigma(i)} =\frac1{Z_P} \left. \left(\prod_{i\in P\setminus T}\partial_{x_i}\right) F_P(\boldsymbol x,\boldsymbol y) \right\|_{x_i=0\ (i\in P)}.$$
  - Explanation: Eliminating non-pin points by identifying variables and extracting a linear coefficient yields a stable polynomial encoding the next-pin permutation with the pin weight, and the generating polynomial of the image of the test set is stable.
- **claim-presence-probabilities** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-pin-presence)): Let $A$ be a random subset of a finite set $I$. Suppose its generating polynomial $G(\boldsymbol y)=\mathbb E\prod_{j\in A}y_j$ is stable. For every $J\subseteq I$, $$\label{eq:pin-presence-product} \Pr(J\subseteq A)\le\prod_{j\in J}\Pr(j\in A).$$ The same assertion holds after conditioning on $B\subseteq A$, whenever that event has positive probability, for subsets of $I\setminus B$.
  - Explanation: If the generating polynomial of a random subset is stable, then the probability that a set is contained in it is at most the product of the individual presence probabilities, also after conditioning.
- **claim-exposure-disintegration** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-exposure)): For $P$ as above, the conditional law of the unexposed picture given $\mathcal H$ under $\mathbb P_P$ is $\mathbb Q_{\mathbf a}(\,\cdot\mid G_P)$, and $g_P(\mathbf a)>0$ for almost every exposure under this pin law.* *More precisely, fix a deterministic initial label set $A\subseteq V_\Lambda$, and let $\mu_A$ be the distribution under $\mathbb P_{\mathrm b}$ of the histories and endpoint mappings of these labels. Let $F_A$ mean that their endpoint set is $A$ and their paths avoid $P$. On $F_A$, let $c_A(\mathbf a)$ be the number of cycles in their endpoint permutation. If $Z_P$ is the normalizer defining $\mathbb P_P$, then for every bounded measurable function $\Phi$ of the exposure and remaining picture, $$\begin{aligned} &\mathbb E_P\Phi(\mathbf a,\omega_{\mathrm h}) \notag\\ &\quad=\frac1{Z_P}\sum_{A\subseteq V_\Lambda} \int_{F_A}2^{c_A(\mathbf a)}\, \mathbb E_{\mathbb Q_{\mathbf a}} [\mathbf 1_{G_P}\Phi(\mathbf a,\omega_{\mathrm h})]\, \mu_A(\,\mathrm d\mathbf a). \label{eq:exposure-disintegration} \end{aligned}$$ Consequently, the exposure marginal on the $A$-branch is $$\frac1{Z_P}\mathbf 1_{F_A}(\mathbf a)2^{c_A(\mathbf a)} g_P(\mathbf a)\,\mu_A(\,\mathrm d\mathbf a). \label{eq:exposure-outer-law}$$ The same conclusions hold with $P$ any finite deterministic pin set.
  - Explanation: After exposing the cycles that miss the pins, the remaining picture has the independent hole law conditioned on every remaining cycle meeting a pin, and the exposure marginal carries an explicit weight.
- **claim-exterior-moments** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-exterior)): Let $\Pi$ be a random permutation matrix on a finite set $V$. Suppose its law is a limit of laws of finite products of independent Bernoulli transpositions and deterministic permutations. Here a Bernoulli transposition equals a fixed transposition with an arbitrary probability $u\in[0,1]$, and equals the identity otherwise. Then, writing $K=\mathbb E\Pi$, $$\mathbb E\bigl[\bigwedge\nolimits^k\Pi\bigr] =\bigwedge\nolimits^k K \qquad (0\le k\le \|V\|). \label{eq:exterior-moments}$$* *In particular, fix an exposure from Proposition <a href="#prop:exposure" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:exposure">12</a> and finitely many time cuts. Under $\mathbb Q_{\mathbf a}$, the permutation on the disjoint union of the hole layers, obtained by following each hole from a cut to the next cyclic cut, satisfies <a href="#eq:exterior-moments" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:exterior-moments">[eq:exterior-moments]</a>.
  - Explanation: For random permutation matrices built from independent Bernoulli transpositions, the expected exterior powers equal the exterior powers of the mean matrix, so expected minors equal minors of the mean.
- **claim-conditional-first-moment** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-first-moment)): Let $V=J\sqcup Q$ be a finite set with $J\ne\varnothing$. Let $\Pi$ be a random permutation matrix satisfying <a href="#eq:exterior-moments" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:exterior-moments">[eq:exterior-moments]</a>, and put $K=\mathbb E\Pi$. Let $G_J$ be the event that every cycle of $\Pi$ meets $J$. On $G_J$, let $\Sigma$ be the column-forward permutation matrix of the next visit to $J$ at positive time along the cycles. Then $$\mathbb P(G_J)=\det(I-K_{QQ}). \label{eq:all-hit-determinant}$$ If this probability is positive, $K_{QQ}$ is transient and $$\mathbb E[\Sigma\mid G_J] =H:=K_{JJ}+K_{JQ}(I-K_{QQ})^{-1}K_{QJ}. \label{eq:conditional-first-moment}$$ The matrix $H$ is the first positive $J$-hitting kernel for the Markov chain with transition matrix $K$, using independent transitions on successive steps. More strongly, for every complex $J\times J$ matrix $W$, $$\mathbb E[\det(I-W\Sigma)\mid G_J]=\det(I-WH). \label{eq:arbitrary-W}$$ An empty $Q$ is allowed, with determinant of the empty matrix equal to one and the Schur correction in <a href="#eq:conditional-first-moment" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:conditional-first-moment">[eq:conditional-first-moment]</a> equal to zero.
  - Explanation: The probability that every cycle meets the pin set is a determinant, and the conditional mean next-pin matrix equals the first-hitting kernel of the mean Markov chain; a determinant version holds for arbitrary matrices.
- **claim-return-identity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-exposure-dense-return)): For $\mathbb P_P$-almost every exposure and every $i\in T$, $$\mathbb P_P\bigl(\text{next pin after }i\text{ lies in }T \mid\mathcal H\bigr) =\mathbb P_{\mathbf a}^{\mathrm{walk}} (\tau_T^+<\tau_E). \label{eq:fresh-return-identity}$$ In particular the first moment in <a href="#eq:exposure-r" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:exposure-r">[eq:exposure-r]</a> is the $\mathbb P_P$-average of the corresponding fresh-turn return probabilities in the exposed environment.
  - Explanation: Given the exposure, the probability that the next pin lies in the test set equals the probability that the fresh-turn walk returns to the test set before reaching the pins, including continuous boundary pins.
- **claim-return-energy** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-return-energy)): For almost every exposure under $\mathbb P_P$, let $U$ be the boundary-killed one-period matrix just defined. There is a smallest nonnegative function $f$ on its cut holes satisfying $$f=1\text{ on }T,\qquad U^*f=f\text{ off }T. \label{eq:minimal-hitting-harmonicity}$$ It is given off $T$ by the probability of ever reaching $T$ on a future turn before boundary killing. In particular $0\le f\le1$, and $f$ is zero on the boundary. With counting inner products, $$\langle f,(I-U)f\rangle =\sum_{i\in T}\bigl(1-(U^*f)_i\bigr), \qquad m(1-r)\ge \mathbb E_P\langle f,(I-U)f\rangle. \label{eq:return-energy}$$ Both $U$ and $f$ in this expectation are functions of the same exposure, with the outer law in <a href="#eq:exposure-outer-law" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:exposure-outer-law">[eq:exposure-outer-law]</a>.
  - Explanation: A minimal hitting function exists for the killed one-period matrix, and the quadratic loss of this function bounds the deficit of the return fraction from above.
- **claim-nongrid-blocking** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-nongrid)): Assume $\mathsf I_p(P)$, and let $t$ be any deterministic time. There is a constant $C_0=C_0(d,\ell)$, uniform in $p\le1$, the volume, the period, the grid, and $P$, such that, for every prescribed finite set $A\subset\mathbb Z^d$ of distinct sites, $$\begin{aligned} \mathbb P_P(A\subseteq\mathcal O_t)&\le C_0p^{2d} &&\text{if }\|A\|=2d, \label{eq:small-blocking}\\ \mathbb P_P(A\subseteq\mathcal O_t)&\le(C_0\sqrt p)^{\|A\|} &&\text{if }A\text{ is a nonempty sparse set of distinct sites}. \label{eq:sparse-blocking} \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: The induction bound on blocked sites at grid times extends to arbitrary deterministic times by controlling the exchange marks in the short interval since the preceding grid time.
- **claim-component-tail** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-component-tail)): There are $p_0>0$ and $c,C,\gamma>0$, depending only on $d,\ell$ and the fixed sparse-growth choices, such that whenever $0<p\le p_0$ and $\mathsf I_p(P)$ holds, $$\label{eq:component-tail} \mathbb P_P\bigl(z\in\mathcal O_t,\ \operatorname{rad}_z\mathcal C_t(z)\ge h\bigr) \le C\exp(-ch^\gamma) \qquad(z\in\mathbb Z^d,\ h\ge1)$$ at every deterministic time $t$. The constants $c,C,\gamma$ are fixed once $p_0$ is fixed and work simultaneously for all $p\le p_0$.
  - Explanation: Blocked components have a stretched-exponential tail in their radius, uniformly for all small enough pinning parameters, proved with separated binary witness trees.
- **claim-irregularity-probability** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-regularity)): Fix $M_*$. Under $\mathsf I_p(P)$, for all $0<p\le p_0$, all deterministic times $t$, and all spatial anchors $x$, $$\label{eq:regularity-failure} \mathbb P_P(t\text{ is not regular for }x) \le \delta:=C R^d p^{2d}+C\exp(-cR^\xi), \qquad \xi=\nu\gamma>0.$$ Here $R\ge1$, and the constants may depend on $M_*$ but are uniform in $p\le p_0$, volume, period, grid, pin set and anchor.
  - Explanation: The probability that a time is not regular for an anchor is bounded by a polynomial term in the scale plus a stretched-exponential term, uniformly in volume and pin set.
- **claim-local-connectivity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-local-connectivity)): Let $d\ge2$ and $K\ge1$ be fixed. There is an integer $\rho=\rho(d,K)\ge K+1$ such that the following holds. If $u,v\in\mathbb Z^d$, $\|u-v\|_\infty\le K$, and $F\subset\mathbb Z^d$ satisfies $$u,v\notin F,\qquad \#(F\cap B_\rho(u))<2d,$$ then a nearest-neighbor path from $u$ to $v$ avoids $F$, lies in $B_\rho(u)$, and has length at most $(2\rho+1)^d$.
  - Explanation: Deleting fewer than twice the dimension of lattice sites near two close surviving points leaves them joined by a short path inside a fixed ball.
- **claim-local-nash** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-local-nash)): Fix $d\ge3$, $\ell$, and $M_*$ as in <a href="#eq:local-radius-choice" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:local-radius-choice">[eq:local-radius-choice]</a>. Let $\mathcal H_t$ be an available-slot graph with at least one and at most $\ell$ slots at every unblocked site, and suppose $t$ is regular for $x$ at scale $R\ge M_*+K_{\mathrm{conn}}+3$. Every nonnegative function $v$ supported on slots above $B_R(x)$ satisfies $$\label{eq:local-nash} \\|v\\|_2^{2+4/d}\le C\mathcal D_t(v)\\|v\\|_1^{4/d},$$ where $C$ depends only on $d,\ell$ and the fixed local radii.
  - Explanation: At a regular time, nonnegative functions supported near the anchor satisfy a Nash norm-energy inequality on the available-slot graph, transferred from the lattice by path comparison.
- **claim-hull-topology** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-hulls)): Let $d\ge2$ and let $\mathcal Z\subset\mathbb Z^d$ be finite. The sets $A_C$ are pairwise disjoint and nearest-neighbor connected. Each $H_C$ is finite, is nearest-neighbor connected, has connected complement, and is contained in the coordinate bounding box of $A_C$. Any two hulls are disjoint or one contains the other.* *For every edge $\{u,v\}$ with $u\in H_C$, $v\notin H_C$, $$\label{eq:hull-clearance} \mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(u,C)=4,\qquad \mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(v,C)=5.$$ Both endpoints are unblocked. The set $S_C$ is connected by steps of sup norm at most one. Each such step can be replaced by a nearest-neighbor path of unblocked sites, contained in the unit enlargement of the bounding box of $A_C$. Consequently any two points of $S_C$ can be joined by a finite path of unblocked sites in that enlargement.
  - Explanation: Filled thickened hulls of blocked components are finite, connected, nested or disjoint, with exact clearance at their boundary cuts and a connected surviving exterior boundary.
- **claim-flow-decay** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-flow-decay)): Let $d\ge3$, $\nu=1/(100d)$, and suppose the anchor $x$ is regular in the sense of Definition <a href="#def:regular-anchor" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:regular-anchor">19</a>. Fix the local constant $M_*$ as in <a href="#eq:local-radius-choice" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:local-radius-choice">[eq:local-radius-choice]</a> and take $R$ sufficiently large in terms of those fixed constants. For every available slot $a$ at any site $y\in B_R(x)$ there is a flow $J_a$ on the available-slot graph such that $$\label{eq:flow-envelope} \operatorname{div}J_a=\mathbf 1_{\{a\}},\qquad \|J_a(e)\|\le C(1+\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(e,y))^{-k}, \qquad k=d-1-d\nu.$$ In particular, $\sum_e J_a(e)^2<\infty$.
  - Explanation: From every available slot near a regular anchor there is a unit flow to infinity on the available-slot graph whose edge values decay polynomially with distance, so it has finite energy.
- **claim-flow-overlap** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-flow-overlap)): Suppose two flows on an available-slot graph obey $$\|J(e)\|\le C_0(1+\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(e,y))^{-k},\qquad \|J'(e)\|\le C_0(1+\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(e,y'))^{-k},$$ where $d/2<k<d$. Then $$\label{eq:flow-overlap} \sum_e \|J(e)J'(e)\| \le C(1+\|y-y'\|_\infty)^{-(2k-d)},$$ where $C$ depends only on $d,\ell,k,C_0$.
  - Explanation: Two flows with polynomial envelopes around different centers have a summed overlap that decays polynomially in the distance between centers.
- **claim-flow-energy** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-flow-energy)): Let $d\ge3$, $\alpha=1/100$, and $\nu=1/(100d)$. Fix $M_*$ as in <a href="#eq:local-radius-choice" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:local-radius-choice">[eq:local-radius-choice]</a> and take $R$ sufficiently large as in Proposition <a href="#prop:flow-decay" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:flow-decay">24</a>. Let $T$ be a finite set of $m$ anchor slots with sites $x_i$, satisfying $$\#\{i\in T:x_i\in B_h(z)\}\le L(1+h)^\alpha \quad\text{for all }z\in\mathbb Z^d,\ h\ge0.$$ Fix an available-slot graph and a subset $I\subset T$ of anchors regular for this graph. For each $i\in I$, let $\mu_i$ be any nonnegative mass on its slots, supported over $B_R(x_i)$ and satisfying $\sum_a\mu_i(a)\le1$. There is a flow $J$ such that $$\label{eq:injection-flow-energy} \operatorname{div}J=\sum_{i\in I}\mu_i, \qquad \sum_e J(e)^2\le C\|I\|R^\alpha\le CmR^\alpha.$$ Consequently every finitely supported real function $g$ on the graph satisfies $$\label{eq:injection-pairing} \left\|\left\langle\sum_{i\in I}\mu_i,g\right\rangle\right\| \le \bigl(CmR^\alpha\mathcal D(g)\bigr)^{1/2}, \qquad \mathcal D(g)=\frac12\sum_e(g(e^-)-g(e^+))^2.$$ All constants are uniform over the choices of $I$ and of the masses.
  - Explanation: For a sparse set of regular anchors with subunit masses, there is a single flow with the combined divergence and energy controlled by the number of anchors and a power of the scale, which bounds the pairing with any function by its Dirichlet energy.
- **claim-two-ends** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-two-ends)): For every fixed exposed environment, $s>0$, and period $\beta\ge4s$, the preceding killed propagators satisfy, for every real vector $v$ on the holes at the distinguished cut with zero boundary values, $$\label{eq:two-ends} \langle v,(I-U)v\rangle\ge\frac12\left( \\|v\\|_2^2-\\|C_{2s}v\\|_2^2 +\\|v\\|_2^2-\\|C_{2s}^{\rm rev}v\\|_2^2\right).$$ Both $u\mapsto\\|C_uv\\|_2$ and $u\mapsto\\|C_u^{\rm rev}v\\|_2$ are nonincreasing.
  - Explanation: The one-period dissipation of a vector is at least half the sum of its squared-norm losses under propagation from the initial end and from the final end.
- **claim-localization** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-localization)): Fix $d$ and $\ell$. There is a constant $C_*=C_*(d,\ell)$ such that for $s\ge1$, $R=\lceil C_*s\rceil$, $\beta\ge5s$, every finite cube, every $E\supseteq D_0$ consisting of $D_0$ and finitely many pins, and every disjoint single-time test $T$, one has $$\label{eq:localization} \mathbb E_P\\|\eta_u-\zeta_u\\|_1\le C m e^{-cs} \qquad(0\le u\le2s)$$ for either direction of propagation. The constants are independent of the volume, the pin sets, $\beta$, and $s$.
  - Explanation: Averaged over the pin law, localized propagation from the test points differs from full propagation in total mass only by an exponentially small amount in the time scale.
- **claim-regular-time-decay** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-regular-time-decay)): Let $T$ satisfy <a href="#eq:leakage-growth" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:leakage-growth">[eq:leakage-growth]</a>, and assume $\mathsf I_p(P)$ for $P=E\cup T$, with $p$ below the preliminary threshold in Proposition <a href="#prop:regularity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:regularity">20</a>. For the localized propagators defined above, in either direction and for $s\le u\le2s$, $$\begin{aligned} \mathbb E_P\\|\eta_{i,u}\\|_2^2 &\le C(s^{-d/2}+\delta)\quad(i\in T), \label{eq:piece-decay}\\ \mathbb E_P\\|\eta_u\\|_2^2 &\le C m\bigl[e^{-cs}+R^\alpha(s^{-d/2}+\delta)\bigr]. \label{eq:all-pieces-decay} \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: After enough regular time the localized propagated mass has small mean squared norm, with a power decay in time plus the irregularity probability.
- **claim-expected-leakage** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-leakage)): Fix the constants in the preceding geometric estimates and choose $C_*$ as in Lemma <a href="#lem:localization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:localization">28</a>. For all sufficiently small $p>0$, every finite cube, every circular grid of mesh at most $p^{4d}$, every $E$ consisting of $D_0$ and grid pins, and every disjoint single-time grid test $T$ satisfying <a href="#eq:leakage-growth" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:leakage-growth">[eq:leakage-growth]</a>, assume $\mathsf I_p(P)$ for $P=E\cup T$. If $\beta\ge5s$, then, for each of the forward and reverse propagations, $$\label{eq:expected-leakage} \mathbb E_P\bigl[\\|f\\|_2^2-\\|C_{2s}f\\|_2^2\bigr] \ge m[1-\varepsilon(p)],$$ where one may take $$\label{eq:epsilon-definition} \varepsilon(p)=C\left[e^{-cs} +R^\alpha\left(s^{-d/2}+\delta+s^{-1}\right)\right], \qquad \delta=C R^d p^{2d}+C e^{-cR^\xi}.$$ All constants are independent of the volume, period, grid, pin sets, and final $p$.
  - Explanation: The hitting function loses nearly all of its test-set squared norm in expectation during each propagation, with an explicit error from localization, diffusion, irregular times and flow pairing.
- **claim-pin-bootstrap** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-pin-bootstrap)): For every integer $d\ge3$ and integer slot multiplicity $\ell\ge1$, there exist $L\ge\max\{2d,\ell,4\}$ and $p_*\in(0,1/4)$ with the following property. Let $0<p\le p_*$ and $\beta\ge5p^{-11/10}$. In any finite cube $\Lambda$, choose a finite circular time grid containing zero and of mesh at most $p^{4d}$. Let $E$ consist of $D_0$ and any subset of the slot points on this grid. Then every nonempty test $T$ at a single grid time, disjoint from $E$ and satisfying <a href="#eq:leakage-growth" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:leakage-growth">[eq:leakage-growth]</a>, obeys $$\label{eq:bootstrap-closed} \mathbb P_E(T\subseteq\mathcal B_E)\le p^{\|T\|}.$$ In particular, with $p=p_*$ and $\beta_0=5p_*^{-11/10}<\infty$, for every finite $\beta\ge\beta_0$, every cube, and every slot $j$ at time zero, $$\label{eq:singleton-missing} \mathbb P_{D_0}(j\in\mathcal B_{D_0})\le p_*.$$ The constants depend only on $d$ and $\ell$.
  - Explanation: A finite downward induction on grid pins shows that all sparse test sets avoid the pins with probability at most a power of a small parameter; in particular a single slot lies on a cycle missing the boundary pins with small probability.
- **claim-surface-cost** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-surface-cost)): For every $N\geq1$, let $G_N$ be the event in the zero-field mark-and-color law that every cycle meeting $D_0$ is colored up. There is a constant $C_{\beta,d,S}<\infty$, independent of $N$, such that $$\begin{aligned} \Pr(G_N)&\geq \exp\{-C_{\beta,d,S}\|\partial\Lambda_N\|\}, \label{eq:boundary-color-cost}\\ \mu_N([SV_N/2,\infty))&\geq \tfrac12\exp\{-C_{\beta,d,S}\|\partial\Lambda_N\|\}. \label{eq:free-spectral-tail} \end{aligned}$$ Conditionally on $G_N$, the uncolored picture has law $\mathbb P_{D_0}$, and the cycles missing $D_0$ still have independent fair colors.
  - Explanation: Coloring all boundary-touching cycles up costs only an exponential in the boundary size, which gives a free-box spectral tail for magnetization above half the maximum with the same surface-order cost.
- **claim-magnetized-limit** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-magnetized-limit)): For $N\geq1$, set $$h_N=N^{-1/2},\qquad K_N=H_N-h_NM_N, \qquad \rho_N^{\mathrm{in}}(C)= \frac{\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(e^{-\beta K_N}C)}{\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(e^{-\beta K_N})} \quad(C\in\mathcal A_{\Lambda_N}).$$ Extend $\rho_N^{\mathrm{in}}$ to a state $\rho_N$ on $\mathcal A$ by the product normalized trace outside $\Lambda_N$, and define $$\label{eq:translation-average} \omega_N(A)=\frac1{V_N}\sum_{x\in\Lambda_N}\rho_N(T_x(A)).$$ There is a subsequence $N_j\to\infty$ on which $\omega_{N_j}$ converges pointwise on $\mathcal A$ to a translation-invariant state $\omega$, and every such subsequential limit satisfies $$\label{eq:positive-limit-magnetization} \omega(S_0^z)\geq S/4.$$
  - Explanation: A field that vanishes slowly with the box size selects the magnetized tail; translation averages then have a subsequential limit that is a translation-invariant state with positive magnetization.
- **claim-deep-dynamics** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-deep-dynamics)): Let $B$ be a local observable, let $L$ be a finite set containing $\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}B$ and the origin, and let $0<T<\infty$. Then $$\label{eq:deep-box-zero-field} \lim_{r\to\infty}\ \sup_{\substack{\Gamma\text{ a finite cube}\\ \mathop{\mathrm{dist}}_\infty(L,\Gamma^c)\geq r}} \ \sup_{\|t\|\leq T}\\|\tau_t^\Gamma(B)-\tau_t(B)\\|=0.$$ Here $\Gamma^c=\mathbb Z^d\setminus\Gamma$. If $$I_N=\{x\in\Lambda_N: \mathop{\mathrm{dist}}_\infty(x,\Lambda_N^c)\geq\lfloor\sqrt N\rfloor\},$$ then $\|\Lambda_N\setminus I_N\|/V_N\to0$ and $$\label{eq:deep-box-with-field} \lim_{N\to\infty}\sup_{x\in I_N}\sup_{\|t\|\leq T} \\|\alpha_t^N(T_x(B))-T_x(\tau_t(B))\\|=0.$$
  - Explanation: Finite-box dynamics, with or without the small field, approximates the infinite-volume dynamics uniformly on compact time intervals for observables deep inside the box.
- **claim-strip-limit** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-strip-limit)): Fix $\beta>0$, and put $\mathcal S_\beta=\{z\in\mathbb C:0\leq\operatorname{Im}z\leq\beta\}$. Let $(F_n)_{n\geq1}$ be functions continuous on $\mathcal S_\beta$, analytic in its interior, and individually bounded there. Suppose a constant $C\geq0$ satisfies $$\|F_n(t)\|\leq C,\qquad \|F_n(t+i\beta)\|\leq C \quad(n\geq1,\ t\in\mathbb R).$$ Then $\|F_n(z)\|\leq C$ throughout $\mathcal S_\beta$. If the two boundary sequences converge locally uniformly on $\mathbb R$ to $f_0$ and $f_\beta$, respectively, then the full sequence $F_n$ converges locally uniformly on the closed strip to a bounded function $F$, continuous on the closed strip and analytic in its interior, with $$\|F(z)\|\leq C,\qquad F(t)=f_0(t),\qquad F(t+i\beta)=f_\beta(t).$$
  - Explanation: Analytic functions on a strip with a common bound on both boundary lines are bounded in the whole strip, and boundary convergence implies convergence on the closed strip to an analytic limit with the limiting boundary values.

{
  "claims": [
    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-main",
        "exact_quote": "For every integer $d\\geq3$ and every $S\\in\\{\\frac12,1,\\frac32,\\ldots\\}$, there is a finite $\\beta_0(d,S)>0$ such that, for every finite $\\beta\\geq\\beta_0(d,S)$, the dynamics associated with <a href=\"#eq:hamiltonian\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:hamiltonian\">[eq:hamiltonian]</a> has a translation-invariant $\\beta$-KMS state $\\omega$ satisfying $$\\omega(S_0^z)\\ge S/4.$$",

      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-slot-realization",
        "exact_quote": "At each site $x$, the operators $\\widehat S_x^a=\\sum_{j=1}^{\\ell}s_{(x,j)}^a$ restrict on $\\operatorname{Sym}^{\\ell}(\\mathbb C^2)$ to the spin-$S$ matrices defined in Section <a href=\"#sec:model\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:model\">1.1</a>. The orthogonal projection onto this subspace is $$\\mathcal S_x=\\frac1{\\ell!}\n      \\sum_{\\kappa\\in\\mathfrak S_\\ell}\\mathcal U_{\\kappa,x}.$$ With $\\mathcal S_\\Lambda=\\prod_{x\\in\\Lambda}\\mathcal S_x$, define $$\\widehat H_\\Lambda\n =-\\sum_{\\{x,y\\}\\subset\\Lambda:\\,|x-y|_1=1}\n          \\sum_{a=x,y,z}\\widehat S_x^a\\widehat S_y^a,\n \\qquad\n \\widehat M_\\Lambda=\\sum_{i\\in V_\\Lambda}s_i^z.$$ Both operators commute with every within-site permutation and with $\\mathcal S_\\Lambda$. Their restrictions to $\\operatorname{ran}\\mathcal S_\\Lambda$ are unitarily equivalent to $H_\\Lambda$ and $M_\\Lambda=\\sum_{x\\in\\Lambda}S_x^z$, respectively. Moreover, $$\\label{eq:slot-hamiltonian}\n \\widehat H_\\Lambda\n   =-\\frac12\\sum_{\\{i,j\\}\\in\\mathcal E_\\Lambda}\n                   \\mathcal U_{(ij)}\n       +\\frac{q_\\Lambda}{4}I.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-271.4"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-spin-loops",
        "exact_quote": "For every finite cube $\\Lambda$, spin $S$, and $0<\\beta<\\infty$ as above, the partition function is $$\\label{eq:loop-partition-function}\n Z_\\Lambda(\\beta):=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}_{\\mathcal H_\\Lambda}e^{-\\beta H_\\Lambda}\n       =e^{\\beta q_\\Lambda/4}\\,\n                     \\mathbb E_{\\mathrm b}2^{c(\\omega)}.$$ More generally, for every function $\\Phi$ on the finite spectrum of $M_\\Lambda$, $$\\label{eq:spectral-loop-law}\n \\frac{\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}_{\\mathcal H_\\Lambda}\n          \\bigl(e^{-\\beta H_\\Lambda}\\Phi(M_\\Lambda)\\bigr)}\n      {Z_\\Lambda(\\beta)}\n =\\frac{\\displaystyle\\mathbb E_{\\mathrm b}\n          \\sum_{\\varepsilon\\in\\{-1,1\\}^{\\{\\text{cycles of }\\omega\\}}}\n          \\Phi\\!\\left(\\frac12\\sum_C\\varepsilon_C a_C\\right)}\n        {\\displaystyle\\mathbb E_{\\mathrm b}2^{c(\\omega)}}.$$ Thus the spectral law of $M_\\Lambda$ is obtained by weighting the base picture by $2^{c(\\omega)}$, then coloring each cycle independently up or down with equal probabilities, and summing the corresponding $+1/2$ or $-1/2$ over its slots at time $0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-271.4"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-prop-spin-loops",

          "source_id": "PM-271.4"
        }

      "exact_statement": "For every finite cube $\\Lambda$, spin $S$, and $0<\\beta<\\infty$ as above, the partition function is $$\\label{eq:loop-partition-function}\n Z_\\Lambda(\\beta):=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}_{\\mathcal H_\\Lambda}e^{-\\beta H_\\Lambda}\n       =e^{\\beta q_\\Lambda/4}\\,\n                     \\mathbb E_{\\mathrm b}2^{c(\\omega)}.$$ More generally, for every function $\\Phi$ on the finite spectrum of $M_\\Lambda$, $$\\label{eq:spectral-loop-law}\n \\frac{\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}_{\\mathcal H_\\Lambda}\n          \\bigl(e^{-\\beta H_\\Lambda}\\Phi(M_\\Lambda)\\bigr)}\n      {Z_\\Lambda(\\beta)}\n =\\frac{\\displaystyle\\mathbb E_{\\mathrm b}\n          \\sum_{\\varepsilon\\in\\{-1,1\\}^{\\{\\text{cycles of }\\omega\\}}}\n          \\Phi\\!\\left(\\frac12\\sum_C\\varepsilon_C a_C\\right)}\n        {\\displaystyle\\mathbb E_{\\mathrm b}2^{c(\\omega)}}.$$ Thus the spectral law of $M_\\Lambda$ is obtained by weighting the base picture by $2^{c(\\omega)}$, then coloring each cycle independently up or down with equal probabilities, and summing the corresponding $+1/2$ or $-1/2$ over its slots at time $0$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-dense-pins",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a finite cube $\\Lambda$, $0<\\beta<\\infty$, and a finite deterministic pin set $F$. Let $(\\mathcal C_n)_{n\\ge1}$ be increasing finite sets of times, each containing $0$, whose union is dense in $\\mathbb R/\\beta\\mathbb Z$, and put $$D_{0,n}=\\partial\\Lambda\\times\\{1,\\ldots,\\ell\\}\\times\\mathcal C_n,\n \\qquad D_n=F\\cup D_{0,n},\\qquad D=F\\cup D_0.$$ For $\\mathbb P_{\\mathrm b}$-almost every picture there is a finite $n_*(\\omega)$ such that, for all $n\\ge n_*(\\omega)$, $$w_{D_n}(\\omega)=w_D(\\omega),\\qquad\n \\mathcal B_{D_n}(\\omega)=\\mathcal B_D(\\omega).$$ The laws $\\mathbb P_{D_n}$ converge to $\\mathbb P_D$ in total variation. More generally, if $\\Phi_n\\to\\Phi$ almost surely under $\\mathbb P_{\\mathrm b}$ and $\\sup_n|\\Phi_n|\\le A<\\infty$, then $$\\label{eq:dense-pin-expectations}\n             \\mathbb E_{D_n}\\Phi_n\\longrightarrow\\mathbb E_D\\Phi.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-271.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-dense-pins",
          "exact_quote": "Fix a finite cube $\\Lambda$, $0<\\beta<\\infty$, and a finite deterministic pin set $F$. More generally, if $\\Phi_n\\to\\Phi$ almost surely under $\\mathbb P_{\\mathrm b}$ and $\\sup_n|\\Phi_n|\\le A<\\infty$, then $$\\label{eq:dense-pin-expectations}\n             \\mathbb E_{D_n}\\Phi_n\\longrightarrow\\mathbb E_D\\Phi.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-271.4"

      "exact_statement": "Fix a finite cube $\\Lambda$, $0<\\beta<\\infty$, and a finite deterministic pin set $F$. More generally, if $\\Phi_n\\to\\Phi$ almost surely under $\\mathbb P_{\\mathrm b}$ and $\\sup_n|\\Phi_n|\\le A<\\infty$, then $$\\label{eq:dense-pin-expectations}\n             \\mathbb E_{D_n}\\Phi_n\\longrightarrow\\mathbb E_D\\Phi.$$",
      "explanation": "Continuous pinning of the boundary is approximated by pinning at finitely many boundary times. Weights, missing-cycle sets and laws agree eventually or converge in total variation.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-factorial",
        "exact_quote": "For every finite pin set $D$, or such a set united with $D_0$, every integer $n\\ge1$, and every nonnegative measurable function $F:X_\\Lambda^n\\to[0,\\infty]$, $$\\label{eq:factorial-domination}\n \\mathbb E_D\\sum_{(u_1,\\ldots,u_n)\\in(\\omega_{\\mathrm m})^n_{\\ne}}\n                 F(u_1,\\ldots,u_n)\n \\le 2^n\\int_{X_\\Lambda^n}F(u_1,\\ldots,u_n)\n                          \\prod_{j=1}^n\\mu(\\,\\mathrm du_j).$$ In particular, the estimate holds with the marks restricted to any specified time interval and with any specified chronological order.",
        "source_id": "PM-271.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-factorial",
          "exact_quote": "For every finite pin set $D$, or such a set united with $D_0$, every integer $n\\ge1$, and every nonnegative measurable function $F:X_\\Lambda^n\\to[0,\\infty]$, $$\\label{eq:factorial-domination}\n \\mathbb E_D\\sum_{(u_1,\\ldots,u_n)\\in(\\omega_{\\mathrm m})^n_{\\ne}}\n                 F(u_1,\\ldots,u_n)\n \\le 2^n\\int_{X_\\Lambda^n}F(u_1,\\ldots,u_n)\n                          \\prod_{j=1}^n\\mu(\\,\\mathrm du_j).$$ In particular, the estimate holds with the marks restricted to any specified time interval and with any specified chronological order.",
          "source_id": "PM-271.4"
        }

      "exact_statement": "For every finite pin set $D$, or such a set united with $D_0$, every integer $n\\ge1$, and every nonnegative measurable function $F:X_\\Lambda^n\\to[0,\\infty]$, $$\\label{eq:factorial-domination}\n \\mathbb E_D\\sum_{(u_1,\\ldots,u_n)\\in(\\omega_{\\mathrm m})^n_{\\ne}}\n                 F(u_1,\\ldots,u_n)\n \\le 2^n\\int_{X_\\Lambda^n}F(u_1,\\ldots,u_n)\n                          \\prod_{j=1}^n\\mu(\\,\\mathrm du_j).$$ In particular, the estimate holds with the marks restricted to any specified time interval and with any specified chronological order.",

        "exact_quote": "For a permutation obtained by a finite product of independent Bernoulli transpositions and deterministic permutations on a finite set $V$, the polynomial $\\mathbb E F_\\pi$ is stable.           "exact_quote": "For a permutation obtained by a finite product of independent Bernoulli transpositions and deterministic permutations on a finite set $V$, the polynomial $\\mathbb E F_\\pi$ is stable. The same conclusion holds for any limit of these permutation laws.*\n\n*Every finite independent product of finite-time Poisson exchange permutations, uniform permutations on specified subsets of $V$, and deterministic permutations has such a limit representation.",
          "source_id": "PM-271.4"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-pin-splicing",
        "exact_quote": "Starting from <a href=\"#eq:pin-layered-symbol\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:pin-layered-symbol\">[eq:pin-layered-symbol]</a>, eliminate each $q\\in V\\setminus P$ by setting $x_q=y_q=t$ and extracting the coefficient of $t$. The resulting nonzero stable polynomial is $$\\label{eq:pin-spliced-symbol}\n F_P(\\boldsymbol x,\\boldsymbol y)\n   =\\mathbb E_{\\mathrm b}\n       \\left[2^{n_P(\\pi)}\\prod_{i\\in P}(x_i+y_{\\sigma(i)})\\right].$$ If $T\\subseteq P$, then the probability generating polynomial of the random subset $\\sigma(T)$ under $\\mathbb P_P$ is stable. More precisely, with $Z_P=\\mathbb E_{\\mathrm b}2^{n_P(\\pi)}>0$, it is $$\\label{eq:pin-all-x-zero}\n G_T(\\boldsymbol y)\n  :=\\mathbb E_P\\prod_{i\\in T}y_{\\sigma(i)}\n   =\\frac1{Z_P}\n     \\left.\n       \\left(\\prod_{i\\in P\\setminus T}\\partial_{x_i}\\right)\n       F_P(\\boldsymbol x,\\boldsymbol y)\n     \\right|_{x_i=0\\ (i\\in P)}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-271.4"
      },

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-pin-splicing",

More precisely, with $Z_P=\\mathbb E_{\\mathrm b}2^{n_P(\\pi)}>0$, it is $$\\label{eq:pin-all-x-zero}\n G_T(\\boldsymbol y)\n  :=\\mathbb E_P\\prod_{i\\in T}y_{\\sigma(i)}\n   =\\frac1{Z_P}\n     \\left.\n       \\left(\\prod_{i\\in P\\setminus T}\\partial_{x_i}\\right)\n       F_P(\\boldsymbol x,\\boldsymbol y)\n     \\right|_{x_i=0\\ (i\\in P)}.$$",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-pin-presence",
        "exact_quote": "Let $A$ be a random subset of a finite set $I$. Suppose its generating polynomial $G(\\boldsymbol y)=\\mathbb E\\prod_{j\\in A}y_j$ is stable. For every $J\\subseteq I$, $$\\label{eq:pin-presence-product}\n           \\Pr(J\\subseteq A)\\le\\prod_{j\\in J}\\Pr(j\\in A).$$ The same assertion holds after conditioning on $B\\subseteq A$, whenever that event has positive probability, for subsets of $I\\setminus B$.",
        "source_id": "PM-271.4"
      },
      "evidence": [

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-exposure",
        "exact_quote": "For $P$ as above, the conditional law of the unexposed picture given $\\mathcal H$ under $\\mathbb P_P$ is $\\mathbb Q_{\\mathbf a}(\\,\\cdot\\mid G_P)$, and $g_P(\\mathbf a)>0$ for almost every exposure under this pin law.*\n\n\n\n*More precisely, fix a deterministic initial label set $A\\subseteq V_\\Lambda$, and let $\\mu_A$ be the distribution under $\\mathbb P_{\\mathrm b}$ of the histories and endpoint mappings of these labels. Let $F_A$ mean that their endpoint set is $A$ and their paths avoid $P$. On $F_A$, let $c_A(\\mathbf a)$ be the number of cycles in their endpoint permutation. If $Z_P$ is the normalizer defining $\\mathbb P_P$, then for every bounded measurable function $\\Phi$ of the exposure and remaining picture, $$\\begin{aligned}\n &\\mathbb E_P\\Phi(\\mathbf a,\\omega_{\\mathrm h}) \\notag\\\\\n &\\quad=\\frac1{Z_P}\\sum_{A\\subseteq V_\\Lambda}\n \\int_{F_A}2^{c_A(\\mathbf a)}\\,\n \\mathbb E_{\\mathbb Q_{\\mathbf a}}\n       [\\mathbf 1_{G_P}\\Phi(\\mathbf a,\\omega_{\\mathrm h})]\\,\n                         \\mu_A(\\,\\mathrm d\\mathbf a).\n \\label{eq:exposure-disintegration}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Consequently, the exposure marginal on the $A$-branch is $$\\frac1{Z_P}\\mathbf 1_{F_A}(\\mathbf a)2^{c_A(\\mathbf a)}\n                       g_P(\\mathbf a)\\,\\mu_A(\\,\\mathrm d\\mathbf a).\n \\label{eq:exposure-outer-law}$$ The same conclusions hold with $P$ any finite deterministic pin set.",
        "source_id": "PM-271.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-exposure",
          "exact_quote": "For $P$ as above, the conditional law of the unexposed picture given $\\mathcal H$ under $\\mathbb P_P$ is $\\mathbb Q_{\\mathbf a}(\\,\\cdot\\mid G_P)$, and $g_P(\\mathbf a)>0$ for almost every exposure under this pin law.*\n\n\n\n*More precisely, fix a deterministic initial label set $A\\subseteq V_\\Lambda$, and let $\\mu_A$ be the distribution under $\\mathbb P_{\\mathrm b}$ of the histories and endpoint mappings of these labels. Let $F_A$ mean that their endpoint set is $A$ and their paths avoid $P$. On $F_A$, let $c_A(\\mathbf a)$ be the number of cycles in their endpoint permutation. If $Z_P$ is the normalizer defining $\\mathbb P_P$, then for every bounded measurable function $\\Phi$ of the exposure and remaining picture, $$\\begin{aligned}\n &\\mathbb E_P\\Phi(\\mathbf a,\\omega_{\\mathrm h}) \\notag\\\\\n &\\quad=\\frac1{Z_P}\\sum_{A\\subseteq V_\\Lambda}\n \\int_{F_A}2^{c_A(\\mathbf a)}\\,\n \\mathbb E_{\\mathbb Q_{\\mathbf a}}\n       [\\mathbf 1_{G_P}\\Phi(\\mathbf a,\\omega_{\\mathrm h})]\\,\n                         \\mu_A(\\,\\mathrm d\\mathbf a).\n \\label{eq:exposure-disintegration}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Consequently, the exposure marginal on the $A$-branch is $$\\frac1{Z_P}\\mathbf 1_{F_A}(\\mathbf a)2^{c_A(\\mathbf a)}\n                       g_P(\\mathbf a)\\,\\mu_A(\\,\\mathrm d\\mathbf a).\n \\label{eq:exposure-outer-law}$$ The same conclusions hold with $P$ any finite deterministic pin set.",
          "source_id": "PM-271.4"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For $P$ as above, the conditional law of the unexposed picture given $\\mathcal H$ under $\\mathbb P_P$ is $\\mathbb Q_{\\mathbf a}(\\,\\cdot\\mid G_P)$, and $g_P(\\mathbf a)>0$ for almost every exposure under this pin law.*\n\n\n\n*More precisely, fix a deterministic initial label set $A\\subseteq V_\\Lambda$, and let $\\mu_A$ be the distribution under $\\mathbb P_{\\mathrm b}$ of the histories and endpoint mappings of these labels. Let $F_A$ mean that their endpoint set is $A$ and their paths avoid $P$. On $F_A$, let $c_A(\\mathbf a)$ be the number of cycles in their endpoint permutation. If $Z_P$ is the normalizer defining $\\mathbb P_P$, then for every bounded measurable function $\\Phi$ of the exposure and remaining picture, $$\\begin{aligned}\n &\\mathbb E_P\\Phi(\\mathbf a,\\omega_{\\mathrm h}) \\notag\\\\\n &\\quad=\\frac1{Z_P}\\sum_{A\\subseteq V_\\Lambda}\n \\int_{F_A}2^{c_A(\\mathbf a)}\\,\n \\mathbb E_{\\mathbb Q_{\\mathbf a}}\n       [\\mathbf 1_{G_P}\\Phi(\\mathbf a,\\omega_{\\mathrm h})]\\,\n                         \\mu_A(\\,\\mathrm d\\mathbf a).\n \\label{eq:exposure-disintegration}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Consequently, the exposure marginal on the $A$-branch is $$\\frac1{Z_P}\\mathbf 1_{F_A}(\\mathbf a)2^{c_A(\\mathbf a)}\n                       g_P(\\mathbf a)\\,\\mu_A(\\,\\mathrm d\\mathbf a).\n \\label{eq:exposure-outer-law}$$ The same conclusions hold with $P$ any finite deterministic pin set.",
      "explanation": "After exposing the cycles that miss the pins, the remaining picture has the independent hole law conditioned on every remaining cycle meeting a pin, and the exposure marginal carries an explicit weight.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-exterior",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\Pi$ be a random permutation matrix on a finite set $V$. Suppose its law is a limit of laws of finite products of independent Bernoulli transpositions and deterministic permutations. Here a Bernoulli transposition equals a fixed transposition with an arbitrary probability $u\\in[0,1]$, and equals the identity otherwise. Then, writing $K=\\mathbb E\\Pi$, $$\\mathbb E\\bigl[\\bigwedge\\nolimits^k\\Pi\\bigr]\n              =\\bigwedge\\nolimits^k K\n       \\qquad (0\\le k\\le |V|).\n \\label{eq:exterior-moments}$$*\n\n*In particular, fix an exposure from Proposition <a href=\"#prop:exposure\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:exposure\">12</a> and finitely many time cuts. Under $\\mathbb Q_{\\mathbf a}$, the permutation on the disjoint union of the hole layers, obtained by following each hole from a cut to the next cyclic cut, satisfies <a href=\"#eq:exterior-moments\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:exterior-moments\">[eq:exterior-moments]</a>.",
        "source_id": "PM-271.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-exterior",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\Pi$ be a random permutation matrix on a finite set $V$. Suppose its law is a limit of laws of finite products of independent Bernoulli transpositions and deterministic permutations. Here a Bernoulli transposition equals a fixed transposition with an arbitrary probability $u\\in[0,1]$, and equals the identity otherwise. Then, writing $K=\\mathbb E\\Pi$, $$\\mathbb E\\bigl[\\bigwedge\\nolimits^k\\Pi\\bigr]\n              =\\bigwedge\\nolimits^k K\n       \\qquad (0\\le k\\le |V|).\n \\label{eq:exterior-moments}$$*\n\n*In particular, fix an exposure from Proposition <a href=\"#prop:exposure\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:exposure\">12</a> and finitely many time cuts. Under $\\mathbb Q_{\\mathbf a}$, the permutation on the disjoint union of the hole layers, obtained by following each hole from a cut to the next cyclic cut, satisfies <a href=\"#eq:exterior-moments\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:exterior-moments\">[eq:exterior-moments]</a>.",
          "source_id": "PM-271.4"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $\\Pi$ be a random permutation matrix on a finite set $V$. Then, writing $K=\\mathbb E\\Pi$, $$\\mathbb E\\bigl[\\bigwedge\\nolimits^k\\Pi\\bigr]\n              =\\bigwedge\\nolimits^k K\n       \\qquad (0\\le k\\le |V|).\n \\label{eq:exterior-moments}$$*\n\n*In particular, fix an exposure from Proposition <a href=\"#prop:exposure\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:exposure\">12</a> and finitely many time cuts. Under $\\mathbb Q_{\\mathbf a}$, the permutation on the disjoint union of the hole layers, obtained by following each hole from a cut to the next cyclic cut, satisfies <a href=\"#eq:exterior-moments\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:exterior-moments\">[eq:exterior-moments]</a>.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-first-moment",
        "exact_quote": "Let $V=J\\sqcup Q$ be a finite set with $J\\ne\\varnothing$. Let $\\Pi$ be a random permutation matrix satisfying <a href=\"#eq:exterior-moments\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:exterior-moments\">[eq:exterior-moments]</a>, and put $K=\\mathbb E\\Pi$. Let $G_J$ be the event that every cycle of $\\Pi$ meets $J$. On $G_J$, let $\\Sigma$ be the column-forward permutation matrix of the next visit to $J$ at positive time along the cycles. Then $$\\mathbb P(G_J)=\\det(I-K_{QQ}).\n \\label{eq:all-hit-determinant}$$ If this probability is positive, $K_{QQ}$ is transient and $$\\mathbb E[\\Sigma\\mid G_J]\n   =H:=K_{JJ}+K_{JQ}(I-K_{QQ})^{-1}K_{QJ}.\n \\label{eq:conditional-first-moment}$$ The matrix $H$ is the first positive $J$-hitting kernel for the Markov chain with transition matrix $K$, using independent transitions on successive steps. More strongly, for every complex $J\\times J$ matrix $W$, $$\\mathbb E[\\det(I-W\\Sigma)\\mid G_J]=\\det(I-WH).\n \\label{eq:arbitrary-W}$$ An empty $Q$ is allowed, with determinant of the empty matrix equal to one and the Schur correction in <a href=\"#eq:conditional-first-moment\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:conditional-first-moment\">[eq:conditional-first-moment]</a> equal to zero.",
        "source_id": "PM-271.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-first-moment",
          "exact_quote": "Let $V=J\\sqcup Q$ be a finite set with $J\\ne\\varnothing$. Let $\\Pi$ be a random permutation matrix satisfying <a href=\"#eq:exterior-moments\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:exterior-moments\">[eq:exterior-moments]</a>, and put $K=\\mathbb E\\Pi$. Let $G_J$ be the event that every cycle of $\\Pi$ meets $J$. On $G_J$, let $\\Sigma$ be the column-forward permutation matrix of the next visit to $J$ at positive time along the cycles. Then $$\\mathbb P(G_J)=\\det(I-K_{QQ}).\n \\label{eq:all-hit-determinant}$$ If this probability is positive, $K_{QQ}$ is transient and $$\\mathbb E[\\Sigma\\mid G_J]\n   =H:=K_{JJ}+K_{JQ}(I-K_{QQ})^{-1}K_{QJ}.\n \\label{eq:conditional-first-moment}$$ The matrix $H$ is the first positive $J$-hitting kernel for the Markov chain with transition matrix $K$, using independent transitions on successive steps. More strongly, for every complex $J\\times J$ matrix $W$, $$\\mathbb E[\\det(I-W\\Sigma)\\mid G_J]=\\det(I-WH).\n \\label{eq:arbitrary-W}$$ An empty $Q$ is allowed, with determinant of the empty matrix equal to one and the Schur correction in <a href=\"#eq:conditional-first-moment\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:conditional-first-moment\">[eq:conditional-first-moment]</a> equal to zero.",
          "source_id": "PM-271.4"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $V=J\\sqcup Q$ be a finite set with $J\\ne\\varnothing$. Let $\\Pi$ be a random permutation matrix satisfying <a href=\"#eq:exterior-moments\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:exterior-moments\">[eq:exterior-moments]</a>, and put $K=\\mathbb E\\Pi$. Let $G_J$ be the event that every cycle of $\\Pi$ meets $J$. On $G_J$, let $\\Sigma$ be the column-forward permutation matrix of the next visit to $J$ at positive time along the cycles. Then $$\\mathbb P(G_J)=\\det(I-K_{QQ}).\n \\label{eq:all-hit-determinant}$$ If this probability is positive, $K_{QQ}$ is transient and $$\\mathbb E[\\Sigma\\mid G_J]\n   =H:=K_{JJ}+K_{JQ}(I-K_{QQ})^{-1}K_{QJ}.\n \\label{eq:conditional-first-moment}$$ The matrix $H$ is the first positive $J$-hitting kernel for the Markov chain with transition matrix $K$, using independent transitions on successive steps. More strongly, for every complex $J\\times J$ matrix $W$, $$\\mathbb E[\\det(I-W\\Sigma)\\mid G_J]=\\det(I-WH).\n \\label{eq:arbitrary-W}$$ An empty $Q$ is allowed, with determinant of the empty matrix equal to one and the Schur correction in <a href=\"#eq:conditional-first-moment\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:conditional-first-moment\">[eq:conditional-first-moment]</a> equal to zero.",
      "explanation": "The probability that every cycle meets the pin set is a determinant, and the conditional mean next-pin matrix equals the first-hitting kernel of the mean Markov chain; a determinant version holds for arbitrary matrices.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-exposure-dense-return",
        "exact_quote": "For $\\mathbb P_P$-almost every exposure and every $i\\in T$, $$\\mathbb P_P\\bigl(\\text{next pin after }i\\text{ lies in }T\n                              \\mid\\mathcal H\\bigr)\n   =\\mathbb P_{\\mathbf a}^{\\mathrm{walk}}\n                         (\\tau_T^+<\\tau_E).\n \\label{eq:fresh-return-identity}$$ In particular the first moment in <a href=\"#eq:exposure-r\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:exposure-r\">[eq:exposure-r]</a> is the $\\mathbb P_P$-average of the corresponding fresh-turn return probabilities in the exposed environment.",
        "source_id": "PM-271.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-exposure-dense-return",
          "exact_quote": "For $\\mathbb P_P$-almost every exposure and every $i\\in T$, $$\\mathbb P_P\\bigl(\\text{next pin after }i\\text{ lies in }T\n                              \\mid\\mathcal H\\bigr)\n   =\\mathbb P_{\\mathbf a}^{\\mathrm{walk}}\n                         (\\tau_T^+<\\tau_E).\n \\label{eq:fresh-return-identity}$$ In particular the first moment in <a href=\"#eq:exposure-r\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:exposure-r\">[eq:exposure-r]</a> is the $\\mathbb P_P$-average of the corresponding fresh-turn return probabilities in the exposed environment.",
          "source_id": "PM-271.4"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For $\\mathbb P_P$-almost every exposure and every $i\\in T$, $$\\mathbb P_P\\bigl(\\text{next pin after }i\\text{ lies in }T\n                              \\mid\\mathcal H\\bigr)\n   =\\mathbb P_{\\mathbf a}^{\\mathrm{walk}}\n                         (\\tau_T^+<\\tau_E).\n \\label{eq:fresh-return-identity}$$ In particular the first moment in <a href=\"#eq:exposure-r\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:exposure-r\">[eq:exposure-r]</a> is the $\\mathbb P_P$-average of the corresponding fresh-turn return probabilities in the exposed environment.",
      "explanation": "Given the exposure, the probability that the next pin lies in the test set equals the probability that the fresh-turn walk returns to the test set before reaching the pins, including continuous boundary pins.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-return-energy",
        "exact_quote": "For almost every exposure under $\\mathbb P_P$, let $U$ be the boundary-killed one-period matrix just defined. There is a smallest nonnegative function $f$ on its cut holes satisfying $$f=1\\text{ on }T,\\qquad U^*f=f\\text{ off }T.\n \\label{eq:minimal-hitting-harmonicity}$$ It is given off $T$ by the probability of ever reaching $T$ on a future turn before boundary killing. In particular $0\\le f\\le1$, and $f$ is zero on the boundary. With counting inner products, $$\\langle f,(I-U)f\\rangle\n        =\\sum_{i\\in T}\\bigl(1-(U^*f)_i\\bigr),\n \\qquad\n m(1-r)\\ge\n       \\mathbb E_P\\langle f,(I-U)f\\rangle.\n \\label{eq:return-energy}$$ Both $U$ and $f$ in this expectation are functions of the same exposure, with the outer law in <a href=\"#eq:exposure-outer-law\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:exposure-outer-law\">[eq:exposure-outer-law]</a>.",
        "source_id": "PM-271.4"
      },

In particular $0\\le f\\le1$, and $f$ is zero on the boundary.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-nongrid",
        "exact_quote": "Assume $\\mathsf I_p(P)$, and let $t$ be any deterministic time.         "source_id": "PM-271.4"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-local-connectivity",
        "exact_quote": "Let $d\\ge2$ and $K\\ge1$ be fixed. There is an integer $\\rho=\\rho(d,K)\\ge K+1$ such that the following holds. If $u,v\\in\\mathbb Z^d$, $|u-v|_\\infty\\le K$, and $F\\subset\\mathbb Z^d$ satisfies $$u,v\\notin F,\\qquad \\#(F\\cap B_\\rho(u))<2d,$$ then a nearest-neighbor path from $u$ to $v$ avoids $F$, lies in $B_\\rho(u)$, and has length at most $(2\\rho+1)^d$.",
        "source_id": "PM-271.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-local-connectivity",
          "exact_quote": "Let $d\\ge2$ and $K\\ge1$ be fixed. There is an integer $\\rho=\\rho(d,K)\\ge K+1$ such that the following holds.           "source_id": "PM-271.4"
        }
      ],

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-hulls",
        "exact_quote": "Let $d\\ge2$ and let $\\mathcal Z\\subset\\mathbb Z^d$ be finite. The sets $A_C$ are pairwise disjoint and nearest-neighbor connected. Each $H_C$ is finite, is nearest-neighbor connected, has connected complement, and is contained in the coordinate bounding box of $A_C$. Any two hulls are disjoint or one contains the other.*\n\n*For every edge $\\{u,v\\}$ with $u\\in H_C$, $v\\notin H_C$, $$\\label{eq:hull-clearance}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(u,C)=4,\\qquad \\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(v,C)=5.$$ Both endpoints are unblocked. The set $S_C$ is connected by steps of sup norm at most one. Each such step can be replaced by a nearest-neighbor path of unblocked sites, contained in the unit enlargement of the bounding box of $A_C$. Consequently any two points of $S_C$ can be joined by a finite path of unblocked sites in that enlargement.",
        "source_id": "PM-271.4"
      },

        "exact_quote": "Let $d\\ge3$, $\\nu=1/(100d)$, and suppose the anchor $x$ is regular in the sense of Definition <a href=\"#def:regular-anchor\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"def:regular-anchor\">19</a>.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-flow-overlap",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose two flows on an available-slot graph obey $$|J(e)|\\le C_0(1+\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(e,y))^{-k},\\qquad\n |J'(e)|\\le C_0(1+\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(e,y'))^{-k},$$ where $d/2<k<d$. Then $$\\label{eq:flow-overlap}\n \\sum_e |J(e)J'(e)|\n \\le C(1+|y-y'|_\\infty)^{-(2k-d)},$$ where $C$ depends only on $d,\\ell,k,C_0$.",

      },

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-flow-energy",
        "exact_quote": "Let $d\\ge3$, $\\alpha=1/100$, and $\\nu=1/(100d)$. Fix $M_*$ as in <a href=\"#eq:local-radius-choice\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:local-radius-choice\">[eq:local-radius-choice]</a> and take $R$ sufficiently large as in Proposition <a href=\"#prop:flow-decay\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:flow-decay\">24</a>. Let $T$ be a finite set of $m$ anchor slots with sites $x_i$, satisfying $$\\#\\{i\\in T:x_i\\in B_h(z)\\}\\le L(1+h)^\\alpha\n \\quad\\text{for all }z\\in\\mathbb Z^d,\\ h\\ge0.$$ Fix an available-slot graph and a subset $I\\subset T$ of anchors regular for this graph. For each $i\\in I$, let $\\mu_i$ be any nonnegative mass on its slots, supported over $B_R(x_i)$ and satisfying $\\sum_a\\mu_i(a)\\le1$. There is a flow $J$ such that $$\\label{eq:injection-flow-energy}\n \\operatorname{div}J=\\sum_{i\\in I}\\mu_i,\n \\qquad \\sum_e J(e)^2\\le C|I|R^\\alpha\\le CmR^\\alpha.$$ Consequently every finitely supported real function $g$ on the graph satisfies $$\\label{eq:injection-pairing}\n \\left|\\left\\langle\\sum_{i\\in I}\\mu_i,g\\right\\rangle\\right|\n \\le \\bigl(CmR^\\alpha\\mathcal D(g)\\bigr)^{1/2},\n \\qquad \\mathcal D(g)=\\frac12\\sum_e(g(e^-)-g(e^+))^2.$$ All constants are uniform over the choices of $I$ and of the masses.",
        "source_id": "PM-271.4"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-two-ends",
        "exact_quote": "For every fixed exposed environment, $s>0$, and period $\\beta\\ge4s$, the preceding killed propagators satisfy, for every real vector $v$ on the holes at the distinguished cut with zero boundary values, $$\\label{eq:two-ends}\n \\langle v,(I-U)v\\rangle\\ge\\frac12\\left(\n   \\|v\\|_2^2-\\|C_{2s}v\\|_2^2\n  +\\|v\\|_2^2-\\|C_{2s}^{\\rm rev}v\\|_2^2\\right).$$ Both $u\\mapsto\\|C_uv\\|_2$ and $u\\mapsto\\|C_u^{\\rm rev}v\\|_2$ are nonincreasing.",
        "source_id": "PM-271.4"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-leakage",
        "exact_quote": "Fix the constants in the preceding geometric estimates and choose $C_*$ as in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:localization\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:localization\">28</a>. For all sufficiently small $p>0$, every finite cube, every circular grid of mesh at most $p^{4d}$, every $E$ consisting of $D_0$ and grid pins, and every disjoint single-time grid test $T$ satisfying <a href=\"#eq:leakage-growth\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:leakage-growth\">[eq:leakage-growth]</a>, assume $\\mathsf I_p(P)$ for $P=E\\cup T$. If $\\beta\\ge5s$, then, for each of the forward and reverse propagations, $$\\label{eq:expected-leakage}\n \\mathbb E_P\\bigl[\\|f\\|_2^2-\\|C_{2s}f\\|_2^2\\bigr]\n             \\ge m[1-\\varepsilon(p)],$$ where one may take $$\\label{eq:epsilon-definition}\n \\varepsilon(p)=C\\left[e^{-cs}\n       +R^\\alpha\\left(s^{-d/2}+\\delta+s^{-1}\\right)\\right],\n \\qquad \\delta=C R^d p^{2d}+C e^{-cR^\\xi}.$$ All constants are independent of the volume, period, grid, pin sets, and final $p$.",

      },

          "exact_quote": "Fix the constants in the preceding geometric estimates and choose $C_*$ as in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:localization\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:localization\">28</a>. If $\\beta\\ge5s$, then, for each of the forward and reverse propagations, $$\\label{eq:expected-leakage}\n \\mathbb E_P\\bigl[\\|f\\|_2^2-\\|C_{2s}f\\|_2^2\\bigr]\n             \\ge m[1-\\varepsilon(p)],$$ where one may take $$\\label{eq:epsilon-definition}\n \\varepsilon(p)=C\\left[e^{-cs}\n       +R^\\alpha\\left(s^{-d/2}+\\delta+s^{-1}\\right)\\right],\n \\qquad \\delta=C R^d p^{2d}+C e^{-cR^\\xi}.$$ All constants are independent of the volume, period, grid, pin sets, and final $p$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-pin-bootstrap",
        "exact_quote": "For every integer $d\\ge3$ and integer slot multiplicity $\\ell\\ge1$, there exist $L\\ge\\max\\{2d,\\ell,4\\}$ and $p_*\\in(0,1/4)$ with the following property. Let $0<p\\le p_*$ and $\\beta\\ge5p^{-11/10}$. In any finite cube $\\Lambda$, choose a finite circular time grid containing zero and of mesh at most $p^{4d}$. Let $E$ consist of $D_0$ and any subset of the slot points on this grid. Then every nonempty test $T$ at a single grid time, disjoint from $E$ and satisfying <a href=\"#eq:leakage-growth\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:leakage-growth\">[eq:leakage-growth]</a>, obeys $$\\label{eq:bootstrap-closed}\n                  \\mathbb P_E(T\\subseteq\\mathcal B_E)\\le p^{|T|}.$$ In particular, with $p=p_*$ and $\\beta_0=5p_*^{-11/10}<\\infty$, for every finite $\\beta\\ge\\beta_0$, every cube, and every slot $j$ at time zero, $$\\label{eq:singleton-missing}\n                  \\mathbb P_{D_0}(j\\in\\mathcal B_{D_0})\\le p_*.$$ The constants depend only on $d$ and $\\ell$.",
        "source_id": "PM-271.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-pin-bootstrap",
          "exact_quote": "For every integer $d\\ge3$ and integer slot multiplicity $\\ell\\ge1$, there exist $L\\ge\\max\\{2d,\\ell,4\\}$ and $p_*\\in(0,1/4)$ with the following property. Let $0<p\\le p_*$ and $\\beta\\ge5p^{-11/10}$. In any finite cube $\\Lambda$, choose a finite circular time grid containing zero and of mesh at most $p^{4d}$. Let $E$ consist of $D_0$ and any subset of the slot points on this grid. Then every nonempty test $T$ at a single grid time, disjoint from $E$ and satisfying <a href=\"#eq:leakage-growth\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:leakage-growth\">[eq:leakage-growth]</a>, obeys $$\\label{eq:bootstrap-closed}\n                  \\mathbb P_E(T\\subseteq\\mathcal B_E)\\le p^{|T|}.$$ In particular, with $p=p_*$ and $\\beta_0=5p_*^{-11/10}<\\infty$, for every finite $\\beta\\ge\\beta_0$, every cube, and every slot $j$ at time zero, $$\\label{eq:singleton-missing}\n                  \\mathbb P_{D_0}(j\\in\\mathcal B_{D_0})\\le p_*.$$ The constants depend only on $d$ and $\\ell$.",
          "source_id": "PM-271.4"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For every integer $d\\ge3$ and integer slot multiplicity $\\ell\\ge1$, there exist $L\\ge\\max\\{2d,\\ell,4\\}$ and $p_*\\in(0,1/4)$ with the following property. Let $0<p\\le p_*$ and $\\beta\\ge5p^{-11/10}$. In any finite cube $\\Lambda$, choose a finite circular time grid containing zero and of mesh at most $p^{4d}$. Let $E$ consist of $D_0$ and any subset of the slot points on this grid. Then every nonempty test $T$ at a single grid time, disjoint from $E$ and satisfying <a href=\"#eq:leakage-growth\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:leakage-growth\">[eq:leakage-growth]</a>, obeys $$\\label{eq:bootstrap-closed}\n                  \\mathbb P_E(T\\subseteq\\mathcal B_E)\\le p^{|T|}.$$ In particular, with $p=p_*$ and $\\beta_0=5p_*^{-11/10}<\\infty$, for every finite $\\beta\\ge\\beta_0$, every cube, and every slot $j$ at time zero, $$\\label{eq:singleton-missing}\n                  \\mathbb P_{D_0}(j\\in\\mathcal B_{D_0})\\le p_*.$$ The constants depend only on $d$ and $\\ell$.",

Let $(F_n)_{n\\geq1}$ be functions continuous on $\\mathcal S_\\beta$, analytic in its interior, and individually bounded there. Suppose a constant $C\\geq0$ satisfies $$|F_n(t)|\\leq C,\\qquad |F_n(t+i\\beta)|\\leq C\n \\quad(n\\geq1,\\ t\\in\\mathbb R).$$ Then $|F_n(z)|\\leq C$ throughout $\\mathcal S_\\beta$. If the two boundary sequences converge locally uniformly on $\\mathbb R$ to $f_0$ and $f_\\beta$, respectively, then the full sequence $F_n$ converges locally uniformly on the closed strip to a bounded function $F$, continuous on the closed strip and analytic in its interior, with $$|F(z)|\\leq C,\\qquad F(t)=f_0(t),\\qquad F(t+i\\beta)=f_\\beta(t).$$",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **state** ([source](#eq:kms-definition)): A state is a positive linear functional $\omega$ on $\mathcal A$ with $\omega(1)=1$.
- **beta-KMS state** ([source](#eq:kms-definition)): For $0<\beta<\infty$, it is a $\beta$-KMS state for $\tau$ if, for every $A,B\in\mathcal A$, there is a bounded function $F_{A,B}$, continuous on the closed strip $0\leq\operatorname{Im}z\leq\beta$ and analytic in its interior, such that $$\label{eq:kms-definition} F_{A,B}(t)=\omega(A\tau_t(B)),\qquad F_{A,B}(t+\mathrm i\beta)=\omega(\tau_t(B)A) \quad(t\in\mathbb R).$$
- **slot set** ([source](#sec:loops)): Set $\ell=2S$ and introduce the *slot set* $$V_\Lambda=\Lambda\times\{1,\ldots,\ell\}.$$
- **pin** ([source](#pm-h-5)): A *pin* is a slot-time point whose cycle is required to have the up color; a cycle meeting a pin has weight one.
- **stable polynomial** ([source](#pm-h-12)): A nonzero polynomial in finitely many complex variables is *stable* if it has no zero when every variable belongs to $\mathbb H$.
- **multiaffine polynomial** ([source](#pm-h-12)): A polynomial is *multiaffine* if its degree in each variable separately is at most one.
- **hole** ([source](#pm-h-16)): At time $t$, let $H_t(\mathbf a)$ be the set of slots not occupied by an exposed label.
- **blocked site** ([source](#pm-h-21)): A site is *blocked* at time $t$ if none of its $\ell$ slots is a hole.
- **regular anchor** ([source](#stmt-def-regular-anchor)): For a spatial anchor $x\in\mathbb Z^d$, call time $t$ *regular for $x$* at scale $R$ if both conditions hold: 1.

<!-- PM:END pm.objects.v2 -->

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-magnetization-lower-bound | spontaneous magnetization of the constructed state | ≥ | $S/4$ | all spins and all low enough temperatures |
| q-beta-threshold | threshold inverse temperature | = | $5p_*^{-11/10}$ | depends only on dimension and slot multiplicity through the pin threshold |
| q-singleton-missing-probability | probability that a time-zero slot lies on a cycle missing the boundary pins | ≤ | $p_*$ | every finite beta at least the threshold, every cube, every slot |
| q-pin-test-bound | simultaneous pin-avoidance probability | ≤ | $(2r)^{\|T\|}$ | r is the mean return fraction |
| q-sparse-alpha | sparse growth exponent | = | $\frac1{100}$ | fixed throughout Sections 5 to 7 |
| q-remote-nu | remote component radius exponent | = | $\frac1{100d}$ | depends on the dimension |
| q-scale-exponent | scale exponent linking the time scale to the pinning parameter | = | $\frac{11}{10}$ | fixed throughout Sections 5 to 7 |
| q-grid-mesh | mesh of the circular time grid | ≤ | $p^{4d}$ | depends on the pinning parameter and dimension |
| q-weight-integral | weighted Young remainder integral | = | $\frac4s$ | time scale s |
| q-leakage-error-small | leakage error | = | $o(p)$ | as the pinning parameter tends to zero |
| q-vanishing-field | selecting field in the cube of radius N | = | $N^{-1/2}$ | all N at least one |
