<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-slot-realization** [claim](#stmt-lem-slot-realization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-slot-realization): Identify symmetric slot states with spin-S states and write the exchange interaction through slot transpositions. -> slot Hamiltonian
- **step-spin-loops** [claim](#stmt-prop-spin-loops) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-spin-loops): Expand the semigroup in Poisson exchange marks and take traces in the product basis. -> loop representation of partition function and magnetization law
- **step-dense-pins** [claim](#stmt-lem-dense-pins) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-dense-pins): Finitely many marks and positive-duration boundary visits make dense finite cuts detect every boundary cycle; dominated convergence. -> finite-grid approximation of boundary pins
- **step-factorial** [claim](#stmt-lem-factorial) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-factorial): Mark insertion changes cycle counts by a bounded amount; iterated Mecke formula. -> factorial domination of exchange marks
- **step-stability-closure** [claim](#stmt-lem-pin-stability-closure) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-pin-stability-closure): Argument principle and a logarithmic derivative sign argument. -> closure operations
- **step-symmetrization** [claim](#stmt-lem-pin-partial-symmetrization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-pin-partial-symmetrization): Two-variable section and an imaginary-part identity. -> stability of mixtures
- **step-exchange-symbol** [claim](#stmt-lem-pin-exchange-symbol) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-pin-exchange-symbol): Iterate partial symmetrization and approximate Poisson and uniform permutations by Bernoulli products. -> stable exchange symbols
- **step-pin-splicing** [claim](#stmt-lem-pin-splicing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-pin-splicing): Delete non-pin vertices by identification and linear coefficient extraction. -> stable generating polynomial of the image of the test set
- **step-presence** [claim](#stmt-lem-pin-presence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-pin-presence): Conditional covariance inequality from stability of a two-variable section. -> product bound on presence probabilities
- **step-pin-test** [claim](#stmt-prop-pin-test) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-pin-presence-2): Combine presence bound, return fraction and comparison of pin laws; pass to continuous boundary by dense cuts. -> simultaneous pin test
- **step-exposure** [claim](#stmt-prop-exposure) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-exposure): Poisson splitting and a pointwise branch identity over initial label sets. -> conditional law of the unexposed picture
- **step-exterior** [claim](#stmt-lem-exterior) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-exterior): Rank-one structure of transpositions makes minors affine, then independent products. -> exterior moment identity
- **step-first-moment** [claim](#stmt-prop-first-moment) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-first-moment): Block elimination and Schur complement on the determinant identity. -> conditional next-pin mean as hitting kernel
- **step-return-identity** [claim](#stmt-lem-exposure-dense-return) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-exposure-dense-return): Finite-cut identification followed by a dense-cut limit. -> fresh-turn walk return identity
- **step-return-energy** [claim](#stmt-lem-return-energy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-return-energy): Monotone finite-horizon hitting functions and minimality. -> return energy bound
- **step-nongrid** [claim](#stmt-lem-nongrid) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-nongrid): Union bound over blocking at the preceding grid time or an incident mark. -> blocking bounds at all times
- **step-component-tail** [claim](#stmt-lem-component-tail) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-component-tail): Separated binary witness trees counted and bounded by the sparse blocking estimate. -> stretched-exponential component tail
- **step-regularity** [claim](#stmt-prop-regularity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-regularity): Union bound for local blocking and summation of component tails. -> irregularity probability bound
- **step-local-connectivity** [claim](#stmt-lem-local-connectivity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-local-connectivity): Connectivity of the lattice minus few sites and a compactness argument for uniform radius. -> short surviving paths
- **step-local-nash** [claim](#stmt-prop-local-nash) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-local-nash): Transfer to a lattice function by path comparison, then averaging over translates. -> local Nash inequality
- **step-hulls** [claim](#stmt-lem-hulls) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-hulls): Thickening, filling, plaquette parity argument for boundary connectivity. -> hull topology and clearance
- **step-flow-decay** [claim](#stmt-prop-flow-decay) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-flow-decay): Orthant flow rerouted around hulls, with congestion count and a local connector. -> decaying unit flow
- **step-flow-overlap** [claim](#stmt-lem-flow-overlap) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-flow-overlap): Dyadic annulus summation of envelopes. -> overlap bound
- **step-flow-energy** [claim](#stmt-prop-flow-energy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-flow-energy): Superpose unit flows and sum overlaps over sparse anchors; Cauchy-Schwarz for the pairing. -> energy bound and pairing inequality
- **step-two-ends** [claim](#stmt-lem-two-ends) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-two-ends): Factor the one-period kernel into contractions and apply Cauchy-Schwarz. -> two-ended dissipation bound
- **step-localization** [claim](#stmt-lem-localization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-localization): Mixed factorial count of exit paths using actual and fresh marks. -> exponentially small localization error
- **step-regular-time-decay** [claim](#stmt-lem-regular-time-decay) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-regular-time-decay): Differential inequality from the Nash inequality during regular time. -> mean squared decay
- **step-leakage** [claim](#stmt-prop-leakage) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-leakage): Weighted Young inequality absorbs the flow pairing into same-time heat energy. -> expected leakage lower bound
- **step-bootstrap** [claim](#stmt-thm-pin-bootstrap) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-pin-bootstrap): Downward induction on grid pins closed by the pin test and the leakage bound. -> uniform pin estimate
- **step-surface-cost** [claim](#stmt-lem-surface-cost) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-surface-cost): Jensen with a cycle count bounded by boundary marks, then a magnetization average from the pin estimate. -> spectral tail with surface cost
- **step-magnetized-limit** [claim](#stmt-prop-magnetized-limit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-magnetized-limit): Tilted Gibbs tail bound, translation average and diagonal compactness. -> translation-invariant magnetized state
- **step-deep-dynamics** [claim](#stmt-lem-deep-dynamics) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-deep-dynamics): Exhaustion argument for fixed times, equicontinuity, and an exact field-rotation formula. -> uniform approximation in deep boxes
- **step-strip-limit** [claim](#stmt-lem-strip-limit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-strip-limit): Maximum principle with a Gaussian factor. -> strip convergence
- **step-main-theorem** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-strip-limit-2): Finite-volume cyclicity of the trace gives both strip boundaries; deep dynamics and the strip lemma give the limit. -> magnetized translation-invariant KMS state

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-main-magnetized-kms-state**: For every dimension at least three and every allowed spin there is a threshold inverse temperature beyond which the zero-field dynamics has a translation-invariant KMS state whose one-site spin expectation is bounded below by a fixed fraction of the spin.
- **inv-uniform-pin-estimate**: Under continuous boundary pinning, a sparse single-time test set of slots lies entirely on cycles missing the pins with probability at most the small parameter raised to the size of the test set; in particular a single slot does so with probability at most the threshold parameter.
- **inv-simultaneous-pin-test**: The probability that all points of a test set lie on cycles missing the existing pins is at most twice the mean return fraction raised to the size of the test set.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-low-dimension**: Quoting the magnetization bound for a one- or two-dimensional lattice. -> The result is proved only for dimension at least three; no ordered state is claimed outside that range.
- **anti-high-temperature**: Applying the magnetization lower bound at temperatures above the threshold temperature. -> The bound is only asserted for inverse temperature at or above a finite threshold; nothing is claimed below it.
- **anti-executable-solver**: Treating the state construction as a numerical routine that outputs the state. -> The state is obtained as a pointwise limit along a subsequence of box sizes, with no rate and no explicit selection rule, so no finite procedure is specified.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main result is an existence statement for an infinite-volume KMS state obtained as a pointwise limit along a subsequence of box sizes (see the magnetized-limit proposition), and the pin estimates are probabilistic bounds on infinite families of laws; no finite constructive procedure with checkable output is stated.

            "exact_quote": "For every integer $d\\geq3$ and every $S\\in\\{\\frac12,1,\\frac32,\\ldots\\}$, there is a finite $\\beta_0(d,S)>0$ such that, for every finite $\\beta\\geq\\beta_0(d,S)$, the dynamics associated with <a href=\"#eq:hamiltonian\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:hamiltonian\">[eq:hamiltonian]</a> has a translation-invariant $\\beta$-KMS state $\\omega$ satisfying $$\\omega(S_0^z)\\ge S/4.$$",
            "source_object": "stmt-thm-main"
          }
        ],
        "id": "inv-main-magnetized-kms-state",

        "statement": "For every dimension at least three and every allowed spin there is a threshold inverse temperature beyond which the zero-field dynamics has a translation-invariant KMS state whose one-site spin expectation is bounded below by a fixed fraction of the spin.",

            "exact_quote": "For every integer $d\\ge3$ and integer slot multiplicity $\\ell\\ge1$, there exist $L\\ge\\max\\{2d,\\ell,4\\}$ and $p_*\\in(0,1/4)$ with the following property. Let $0<p\\le p_*$ and $\\beta\\ge5p^{-11/10}$. In any finite cube $\\Lambda$, choose a finite circular time grid containing zero and of mesh at most $p^{4d}$. Let $E$ consist of $D_0$ and any subset of the slot points on this grid. Then every nonempty test $T$ at a single grid time, disjoint from $E$ and satisfying <a href=\"#eq:leakage-growth\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:leakage-growth\">[eq:leakage-growth]</a>, obeys $$\\label{eq:bootstrap-closed}\n                  \\mathbb P_E(T\\subseteq\\mathcal B_E)\\le p^{|T|}.$$ In particular, with $p=p_*$ and $\\beta_0=5p_*^{-11/10}<\\infty$, for every finite $\\beta\\ge\\beta_0$, every cube, and every slot $j$ at time zero, $$\\label{eq:singleton-missing}\n                  \\mathbb P_{D_0}(j\\in\\mathcal B_{D_0})\\le p_*.$$ The constants depend only on $d$ and $\\ell$.",

          }

      },

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper proves existence of a magnetized equilibrium state through compactness arguments; it specifies no API, data format or executable component that a program could implement. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The random-loop picture resembles a stirring simulation, but the paper gives no sampling procedure, update rule or cost analysis, so nothing is directly implementable here. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The limit state is obtained along an unspecified subsequence, so no tool can compute it or check the bound on it. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The downward induction on grid pins is a proof device over a finite family of pin sets, not an algorithm with inputs, outputs and a running time. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The filled hulls of blocked components are geometric objects used to route flows in the analysis; no data structure or query interface is proposed. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper contains no computation model, kernel or cost analysis; its measure-theoretic bounds say nothing about accelerator design. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No hardness assumption, adversary or security reduction appears; random subsets and negative dependence are used only for probability bounds on loops. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The determinant and Markov-kernel identities are exact statements about random permutation matrices in this model; they involve no learning, data or generalization claim. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Flows with controlled energy are constructed only to bound a pairing inside a proof; there is no objective, constraint set or solver for an optimisation task. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Finite-volume Poisson exchange pictures and their boundary pins are a mathematical model of a quantum spin system, not a protocol with processes, messages or faults. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result belongs to mathematical physics: an existence theorem for a magnetized equilibrium state in dimensions at least three. It supports theoretical understanding, not a direct engineering construction. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-dimension-three** (hypothesis): The magnetization conclusion is asserted only for lattice dimension at least three. Nothing is claimed in lower dimensions, where the paper recalls an impossibility theorem. Blocked use: ['Quoting the magnetization bound for a one- or two-dimensional lattice', 'Extending the construction to dimension below three']
- **limit-low-temperature-threshold** (hypothesis): The state exists only for inverse temperature at or above a finite threshold depending on dimension and spin. No claim is made at or near higher temperatures. Blocked use: ['Applying the lower bound on magnetization at inverse temperatures below the threshold', 'Reading the threshold as a sharp critical value']
- **limit-subsequence-nonconstructive** (nonconstructive): The magnetized state is obtained as a pointwise limit along a subsequence of box sizes, with no rule for choosing the subsequence and no rate of convergence. Blocked use: ['Treating the construction as an algorithm that outputs the state', 'Claiming uniqueness of the limit state or convergence of the full sequence']
- **limit-chosen-state-not-average** (formal_scope): The magnetization belongs to the chosen state. It need not persist in a rotation-invariant average of states. Blocked use: ['Assuming a rotation-invariant Gibbs state has nonzero one-site spin expectation']
- **limit-pin-estimate-regime** (hypothesis): The uniform pin estimate requires a small pinning parameter, a period bounded below by a power of its inverse, a finite grid of small mesh, and test sets satisfying the sparse growth condition. It holds for sparse single-time tests only. Blocked use: ['Applying the estimate to dense or multi-time test sets', 'Using it with a period shorter than the stated lower bound']
- **limit-simultaneous-test-scope** (hypothesis): The simultaneous pin test bounds avoidance by a power of twice the mean return fraction. It is informative only when that fraction is small, and it needs the test set to be single-time and disjoint from the pins. Blocked use: ['Using the bound when the return fraction is at least one half', 'Applying it to test sets that intersect the existing pins']
- **limit-nearest-neighbor-model** (formal_scope): All results concern the nearest-neighbor isotropic ferromagnetic Hamiltonian on cubic lattice boxes. Other couplings, lattices or anisotropy are not covered. Blocked use: ['Transferring the bound to anisotropic or long-range interactions', 'Transferring the bound to non-cubic lattices']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 271 (`lean/docs/271.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Spontaneous magnetization in the quantum Heisenberg ferromagnet](../../preprints/Spontaneous-magnetization-in-the-quantum-Heisenberg-ferromagnet-September-24-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The formalization proves spontaneous magnetization for the nearest-neighbor isotropic quantum Heisenberg ferromagnet on $\mathbb Z^d$ for every $d\ge3$ and every spin $S\in\{\tfrac12,1,\tfrac32,\ldots\}$. At every sufficiently low positive temperature, it constructs a translation-invariant equilibrium state satisfying the KMS condition for the zero-field dynamics and having magnetization at least $S/4$.
>
> It also proves convergence of the finite-volume dynamics to the infinite-volume dynamics used in the KMS statement.

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

noncomputable section

universe u v


open scoped ComplexConjugate ENNReal Classical

namespace Heisenberg

abbrev L2 (ι : Type u) := lp (fun _ : ι => ℂ) 2

def l2Reindex {ι : Type u} {κ : Type v} (e : ι ≃ κ) : L2 ι ≃ₗᵢ[ℂ] L2 κ where
  toFun f := ⟨fun j => f (e.symm j), by
    apply memℓp_gen
    exact e.symm.summable_iff.mpr ((lp.memℓp f).summable (by norm_num))⟩
  invFun f := ⟨fun i => f (e i), by
    apply memℓp_gen
    exact e.summable_iff.mpr ((lp.memℓp f).summable (by norm_num))⟩
  left_inv f := by ext i; simp
  right_inv f := by ext j; simp
  map_add' f g := by ext j; rfl
  map_smul' c f := by ext j; rfl
  norm_map' f := by
    rw [lp.norm_eq_tsum_rpow (by norm_num), lp.norm_eq_tsum_rpow (by norm_num)]
    congr 1
    exact e.symm.tsum_eq (fun i => ‖f i‖ ^ (2 : ℝ≥0∞).toReal)

def l2Projection {ι : Type u} (p : ι → Prop) : L2 ι →L[ℂ] L2 ι := by
  classical
  let f : L2 ι →ₗ[ℂ] L2 ι :=
    { toFun := fun v => ⟨fun i => if p i then v i else 0,
        (lp.memℓp v).mono' (fun i => by split_ifs <;> simp)⟩
      map_add' := fun v w => by
        ext i
        change (if p i then v i + w i else 0) =
          (if p i then v i else 0) + (if p i then w i else 0)
        by_cases h : p i <;> simp [h]
      map_smul' := fun c v => by ext i; by_cases h : p i <;> simp [h] }
  exact f.mkContinuous 1 (fun v => by
    simpa only [one_mul] using lp.norm_mono (by norm_num : (2 : ℝ≥0∞) ≠ 0)
      (x := f v) (y := v) (fun i => by
        change ‖if p i then v i else 0‖ ≤ ‖v i‖
        split_ifs <;> simp))

abbrev Site (d : ℕ) := Fin d → ℤ
abbrev Level (ℓ : ℕ) := Fin (ℓ + 1)
abbrev Configuration (d ℓ : ℕ) := Site d →₀ Level ℓ
abbrev SpinHilbert (d ℓ : ℕ) := L2 (Configuration d ℓ)
abbrev SpinOperator (d ℓ : ℕ) := SpinHilbert d ℓ →L[ℂ] SpinHilbert d ℓ

def configPerm (d ℓ : ℕ) (x : Site d) (σ : Equiv.Perm (Level ℓ)) :
    Equiv.Perm (Configuration d ℓ) where
  toFun c := c.update x (σ (c x))
  invFun c := c.update x (σ.symm (c x))
  left_inv c := by
    classical
    ext y
    by_cases h : y = x <;> simp [Finsupp.update_apply, h]
  right_inv c := by
    classical
    ext y
    by_cases h : y = x <;> simp [Finsupp.update_apply, h]

def matrixUnit (d ℓ : ℕ) (x : Site d) (a b : Level ℓ) : SpinOperator d ℓ :=
  (l2Projection (fun c : Configuration d ℓ => c x = a)).comp
    (l2Reindex (configPerm d ℓ x (Equiv.swap a b))).toContinuousLinearEquiv.toContinuousLinearMap

def operatorRelabel (d ℓ : ℕ) (e : Equiv.Perm (Site d)) :
    SpinOperator d ℓ ≃⋆ₐ[ℂ] SpinOperator d ℓ :=
  (l2Reindex (Finsupp.equivCongrLeft e)).conjStarAlgEquiv

def quasiLocalAlgebra (d ℓ : ℕ) : StarSubalgebra ℂ (SpinOperator d ℓ) :=
  (StarAlgebra.adjoin ℂ {A | ∃ x a b, A = matrixUnit d ℓ x a b}).topologicalClosure

abbrev QuasiLocal (d ℓ : ℕ) := quasiLocalAlgebra d ℓ

def localUnit (d ℓ : ℕ) (x : Site d) (a b : Level ℓ) : QuasiLocal d ℓ :=
  ⟨matrixUnit d ℓ x a b, (StarSubalgebra.le_topologicalClosure _)
    (StarAlgebra.subset_adjoin ℂ _ ⟨x, a, b, rfl⟩)⟩

def translation (d ℓ : ℕ) (x : Site d) : QuasiLocal d ℓ →⋆ₐ[ℂ] QuasiLocal d ℓ := by
  have l2Reindex_apply {ι : Type} {κ : Type} (e : ι ≃ κ) (f : L2 ι) (j : κ) :
      l2Reindex e f j = f (e.symm j) := rfl
  have l2Projection_apply {ι : Type} (p : ι → Prop) (v : L2 ι) (i : ι) :
      l2Projection p v i = if p i then v i else 0 := by classical rfl
  have matrixUnit_apply (d ℓ : ℕ) (x : Site d) (a b : Level ℓ)
      (v : SpinHilbert d ℓ) (c : Configuration d ℓ) :
      matrixUnit d ℓ x a b v c = if c x = a then v (c.update x b) else 0 := by
    simp only [matrixUnit, ContinuousLinearMap.comp_apply, l2Projection_apply]
    split_ifs with h
    · change v (c.update x ((Equiv.swap a b).symm (c x))) = v (c.update x b)
      simp [h]
    · rfl
  have l2Reindex_symm_apply {ι : Type} {κ : Type} (e : ι ≃ κ) (v : L2 κ) (i : ι) :
      (l2Reindex e).symm v i = v (e i) := rfl
  have relabel_matrixUnit (d ℓ : ℕ) (e : Equiv.Perm (Site d))
      (x : Site d) (a b : Level ℓ) :
      operatorRelabel d ℓ e (matrixUnit d ℓ x a b) = matrixUnit d ℓ (e x) a b := by
    ext v c
    simp only [operatorRelabel, LinearIsometryEquiv.conjStarAlgEquiv_apply_apply,
      l2Reindex_apply, matrixUnit_apply]
    change (if c (e x) = a then
      (l2Reindex (Finsupp.equivCongrLeft e)).symm v
        (((Finsupp.equivCongrLeft e).symm c).update x b) else 0) = _
    rw [l2Reindex_symm_apply]
    by_cases h : c (e x) = a
    · simp only [h, ite_eq_left]
      apply congrArg v
      ext y
      simp only [Finsupp.equivCongrLeft_apply, Finsupp.equivMapDomain_apply,
        Finsupp.update_apply]
      by_cases hy : y = e x
      · subst y; simp
      · have hy' : e.symm y ≠ x := fun hxy => hy (by simpa using congrArg e hxy)
        simp [hy, hy']
    · simp only [h, ite_false]
  have relabel_mem (d ℓ : ℕ) (e : Equiv.Perm (Site d))
      {A : SpinOperator d ℓ} (hA : A ∈ quasiLocalAlgebra d ℓ) :
      operatorRelabel d ℓ e A ∈ quasiLocalAlgebra d ℓ := by
    let f := (operatorRelabel d ℓ e).toStarAlgHom
    have h : StarAlgebra.adjoin ℂ {A | ∃ x a b, A = matrixUnit d ℓ x a b} ≤
        (quasiLocalAlgebra d ℓ).comap f := by
      apply StarAlgebra.adjoin_le
      rintro _ ⟨x, a, b, rfl⟩
      change operatorRelabel d ℓ e (matrixUnit d ℓ x a b) ∈ quasiLocalAlgebra d ℓ
      rw [relabel_matrixUnit]
      exact (localUnit d ℓ (e x) a b).property
    have hc : IsClosed ((quasiLocalAlgebra d ℓ).comap f : Set (SpinOperator d ℓ)) :=
      (StarSubalgebra.isClosed_topologicalClosure _).preimage
        (StarAlgEquiv.isometry (operatorRelabel d ℓ e)).continuous
    exact StarSubalgebra.topologicalClosure_minimal h hc hA
  exact let f := (operatorRelabel d ℓ (Equiv.addLeft x)).toStarAlgHom
    (f.comp (quasiLocalAlgebra d ℓ).subtype).codRestrict (quasiLocalAlgebra d ℓ)
      (fun A => relabel_mem d ℓ (Equiv.addLeft x) A.property)

def magneticLevel (ℓ : ℕ) (k : Level ℓ) : ℝ := (k : ℝ) - (ℓ : ℝ) / 2

def spinZ (d ℓ : ℕ) (x : Site d) : QuasiLocal d ℓ :=
  ∑ k : Level ℓ, (magneticLevel ℓ k : ℂ) • localUnit d ℓ x k k

def spinPlus (d ℓ : ℕ) (x : Site d) : QuasiLocal d ℓ :=
  ∑ k : Fin ℓ,
    (Real.sqrt ((ℓ / 2 : ℝ) * (ℓ / 2 + 1) -
      magneticLevel ℓ k.castSucc * (magneticLevel ℓ k.castSucc + 1)) : ℂ) •
      localUnit d ℓ x k.succ k.castSucc

def spinMinus (d ℓ : ℕ) (x : Site d) : QuasiLocal d ℓ := star (spinPlus d ℓ x)

def spinX (d ℓ : ℕ) (x : Site d) : QuasiLocal d ℓ :=
  (1 / 2 : ℂ) • (spinPlus d ℓ x + spinMinus d ℓ x)

def spinY (d ℓ : ℕ) (x : Site d) : QuasiLocal d ℓ :=
  (1 / (2 * Complex.I) : ℂ) • (spinPlus d ℓ x - spinMinus d ℓ x)

def NearestNeighbor {d : ℕ} (x y : Site d) : Prop :=
  ∑ i : Fin d, |x i - y i| = (1 : ℤ)

def volumeEdges {d : ℕ} (Λ : Finset (Site d)) : Finset (Site d × Site d) :=
  (Λ ×ˢ Λ).filter fun p => NearestNeighbor p.1 p.2 ∧ WellOrderingRel p.1 p.2

def hamiltonian (d ℓ : ℕ) (Λ : Finset (Site d)) : QuasiLocal d ℓ :=
  -∑ e ∈ volumeEdges Λ,
    (spinX d ℓ e.1 * spinX d ℓ e.2 + spinY d ℓ e.1 * spinY d ℓ e.2 +
      spinZ d ℓ e.1 * spinZ d ℓ e.2)

def box (d n : ℕ) : Finset (Site d) :=
  Fintype.piFinset fun _ : Fin d => Finset.Icc (-(n : ℤ)) (n : ℤ)

def finiteDynamics (d ℓ : ℕ) (Λ : Finset (Site d)) (t : ℝ)
    (A : QuasiLocal d ℓ) : QuasiLocal d ℓ :=
  NormedSpace.exp ((Complex.I * (t : ℂ)) • hamiltonian d ℓ Λ) * A *
    NormedSpace.exp ((-(Complex.I * (t : ℂ))) • hamiltonian d ℓ Λ)

def dynamics (d ℓ : ℕ) (t : ℝ) (A : QuasiLocal d ℓ) : QuasiLocal d ℓ :=
  Filter.limUnder Filter.atTop (fun n : ℕ => finiteDynamics d ℓ (box d n) t A)

def DynamicsConverges (d ℓ : ℕ) : Prop :=
  ∀ (t : ℝ) (A : QuasiLocal d ℓ),
    Filter.Tendsto (fun n : ℕ => finiteDynamics d ℓ (box d n) t A)
      Filter.atTop (nhds (dynamics d ℓ t A))

structure State (d ℓ : ℕ) where
  functional : QuasiLocal d ℓ →L[ℂ] ℂ
  normalized : functional 1 = 1
  positive : ∀ A, (functional (star A * A)).im = 0 ∧
    0 ≤ (functional (star A * A)).re

def TranslationInvariant {d ℓ : ℕ} (ω : State d ℓ) : Prop :=
  ∀ (x : Site d) (A : QuasiLocal d ℓ), ω.functional (translation d ℓ x A) = ω.functional A

def IsKMS {d ℓ : ℕ} (β : ℝ) (ω : State d ℓ) : Prop :=
  0 < β ∧ ∀ A B : QuasiLocal d ℓ, ∃ F : ℂ → ℂ,
    ContinuousOn F {z | 0 ≤ z.im ∧ z.im ≤ β} ∧
    DifferentiableOn ℂ F {z | 0 < z.im ∧ z.im < β} ∧
    (∃ C : ℝ, ∀ z : ℂ, 0 ≤ z.im → z.im ≤ β → ‖F z‖ ≤ C) ∧
    (∀ t : ℝ, F t = ω.functional (A * dynamics d ℓ t B)) ∧
    (∀ t : ℝ, F ((t : ℂ) + Complex.I * β) = ω.functional (dynamics d ℓ t B * A))

open scoped Classical BigOperators ComplexConjugate
open Filter Topology

theorem spontaneous_magnetization (d ℓ : ℕ) (hd : 3≤d) (hℓ : 1≤ℓ) :
    DynamicsConverges d ℓ ∧
      ∃ β₀ : ℝ,0<β₀ ∧ ∀ β : ℝ,β₀≤β →
        ∃ ω : State d ℓ,TranslationInvariant ω ∧ IsKMS β ω ∧
          (ω.functional (spinZ d ℓ 0)).im=0 ∧
          (ℓ:ℝ)/8≤(ω.functional (spinZ d ℓ 0)).re := by
  sorry

end Heisenberg

end

end OAI
```

    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "l2Reindex",
      "statement_lean": "def l2Reindex {ι : Type u} {κ : Type v} (e : ι ≃ κ) : L2 ι ≃ₗᵢ[ℂ] L2 κ where\n  toFun f := ⟨fun j => f (e.symm j), by\n    apply memℓp_gen\n    exact e.symm.summable_iff.mpr ((lp.memℓp f).summable (by norm_num))⟩\n  invFun f := ⟨fun i => f (e i), by\n    apply memℓp_gen\n    exact e.summable_iff.mpr ((lp.memℓp f).summable (by norm_num))⟩\n  left_inv f := by ext i; simp\n  right_inv f := by ext j; simp\n  map_add' f g := by ext j; rfl\n  map_smul' c f := by ext j; rfl\n  norm_map' f := by\n    rw [lp.norm_eq_tsum_rpow (by norm_num), lp.norm_eq_tsum_rpow (by norm_num)]\n    congr 1\n    exact e.symm.tsum_eq (fun i => ‖f i‖ ^ (2 : ℝ≥0∞).toReal)"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "l2Projection",
      "statement_lean": "def l2Projection {ι : Type u} (p : ι → Prop) : L2 ι →L[ℂ] L2 ι := by\n  classical\n  let f : L2 ι →ₗ[ℂ] L2 ι :=\n    { toFun := fun v => ⟨fun i => if p i then v i else 0,\n        (lp.memℓp v).mono' (fun i => by split_ifs <;> simp)⟩\n      map_add' := fun v w => by\n        ext i\n        change (if p i then v i + w i else 0) =\n          (if p i then v i else 0) + (if p i then w i else 0)\n        by_cases h : p i <;> simp [h]\n      map_smul' := fun c v => by ext i; by_cases h : p i <;> simp [h] }\n  exact f.mkContinuous 1 (fun v => by\n    simpa only [one_mul] using lp.norm_mono (by norm_num : (2 : ℝ≥0∞) ≠ 0)\n      (x := f v) (y := v) (fun i => by\n        change ‖if p i then v i else 0‖ ≤ ‖v i‖\n        split_ifs <;> simp))"
    },

      "statement_lean": "abbrev SpinHilbert (d ℓ : ℕ) := L2 (Configuration d ℓ)"

      "docstring": null,

      "statement_lean": "abbrev SpinOperator (d ℓ : ℕ) := SpinHilbert d ℓ →L[ℂ] SpinHilbert d ℓ"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "configPerm",
      "statement_lean": "def configPerm (d ℓ : ℕ) (x : Site d) (σ : Equiv.Perm (Level ℓ)) :\n    Equiv.Perm (Configuration d ℓ) where\n  toFun c := c.update x (σ (c x))\n  invFun c := c.update x (σ.symm (c x))\n  left_inv c := by\n    classical\n    ext y\n    by_cases h : y = x <;> simp [Finsupp.update_apply, h]\n  right_inv c := by\n    classical\n    ext y\n    by_cases h : y = x <;> simp [Finsupp.update_apply, h]"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "matrixUnit",
      "statement_lean": "def matrixUnit (d ℓ : ℕ) (x : Site d) (a b : Level ℓ) : SpinOperator d ℓ :=\n  (l2Projection (fun c : Configuration d ℓ => c x = a)).comp\n    (l2Reindex (configPerm d ℓ x (Equiv.swap a b))).toContinuousLinearEquiv.toContinuousLinearMap"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "operatorRelabel",
      "statement_lean": "def operatorRelabel (d ℓ : ℕ) (e : Equiv.Perm (Site d)) :\n    SpinOperator d ℓ ≃⋆ₐ[ℂ] SpinOperator d ℓ :=\n  (l2Reindex (Finsupp.equivCongrLeft e)).conjStarAlgEquiv"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "quasiLocalAlgebra",
      "statement_lean": "def quasiLocalAlgebra (d ℓ : ℕ) : StarSubalgebra ℂ (SpinOperator d ℓ) :=\n  (StarAlgebra.adjoin ℂ {A | ∃ x a b, A = matrixUnit d ℓ x a b}).topologicalClosure"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "abbrev",
      "name": "QuasiLocal",
      "statement_lean": "abbrev QuasiLocal (d ℓ : ℕ) := quasiLocalAlgebra d ℓ"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "localUnit",
      "statement_lean": "def localUnit (d ℓ : ℕ) (x : Site d) (a b : Level ℓ) : QuasiLocal d ℓ :=\n  ⟨matrixUnit d ℓ x a b, (StarSubalgebra.le_topologicalClosure _)\n    (StarAlgebra.subset_adjoin ℂ _ ⟨x, a, b, rfl⟩)⟩"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "translation",
      "statement_lean": "def translation (d ℓ : ℕ) (x : Site d) : QuasiLocal d ℓ →⋆ₐ[ℂ] QuasiLocal d ℓ := by\n  have l2Reindex_apply {ι : Type} {κ : Type} (e : ι ≃ κ) (f : L2 ι) (j : κ) :\n      l2Reindex e f j = f (e.symm j) := rfl\n  have l2Projection_apply {ι : Type} (p : ι → Prop) (v : L2 ι) (i : ι) :\n      l2Projection p v i = if p i then v i else 0 := by classical rfl\n  have matrixUnit_apply (d ℓ : ℕ) (x : Site d) (a b : Level ℓ)\n      (v : SpinHilbert d ℓ) (c : Configuration d ℓ) :\n      matrixUnit d ℓ x a b v c = if c x = a then v (c.update x b) else 0 := by\n    simp only [matrixUnit, ContinuousLinearMap.comp_apply, l2Projection_apply]\n    split_ifs with h\n    · change v (c.update x ((Equiv.swap a b).symm (c x))) = v (c.update x b)\n      simp [h]\n    · rfl\n  have l2Reindex_symm_apply {ι : Type} {κ : Type} (e : ι ≃ κ) (v : L2 κ) (i : ι) :\n      (l2Reindex e).symm v i = v (e i) := rfl\n  have relabel_matrixUnit (d ℓ : ℕ) (e : Equiv.Perm (Site d))\n      (x : Site d) (a b : Level ℓ) :\n      operatorRelabel d ℓ e (matrixUnit d ℓ x a b) = matrixUnit d ℓ (e x) a b := by\n    ext v c\n    simp only [operatorRelabel, LinearIsometryEquiv.conjStarAlgEquiv_apply_apply,\n      l2Reindex_apply, matrixUnit_apply]\n    change (if c (e x) = a then\n      (l2Reindex (Finsupp.equivCongrLeft e)).symm v\n        (((Finsupp.equivCongrLeft e).symm c).update x b) else 0) = _\n    rw [l2Reindex_symm_apply]\n    by_cases h : c (e x) = a\n    · simp only [h, ite_eq_left]\n      apply congrArg v\n      ext y\n      simp only [Finsupp.equivCongrLeft_apply, Finsupp.equivMapDomain_apply,\n        Finsupp.update_apply]\n      by_cases hy : y = e x\n      · subst y; simp\n      · have hy' : e.symm y ≠ x := fun hxy => hy (by simpa using congrArg e hxy)\n        simp [hy, hy']\n    · simp only [h, ite_false]\n  have relabel_mem (d ℓ : ℕ) (e : Equiv.Perm (Site d))\n      {A : SpinOperator d ℓ} (hA : A ∈ quasiLocalAlgebra d ℓ) :\n      operatorRelabel d ℓ e A ∈ quasiLocalAlgebra d ℓ := by\n    let f := (operatorRelabel d ℓ e).toStarAlgHom\n    have h : StarAlgebra.adjoin ℂ {A | ∃ x a b, A = matrixUnit d ℓ x a b} ≤\n        (quasiLocalAlgebra d ℓ).comap f := by\n      apply StarAlgebra.adjoin_le\n      rintro _ ⟨x, a, b, rfl⟩\n      change operatorRelabel d ℓ e (matrixUnit d ℓ x a b) ∈ quasiLocalAlgebra d ℓ\n      rw [relabel_matrixUnit]\n      exact (localUnit d ℓ (e x) a b).property\n    have hc : IsClosed ((quasiLocalAlgebra d ℓ).comap f : Set (SpinOperator d ℓ)) :=\n      (StarSubalgebra.isClosed_topologicalClosure _).preimage\n        (StarAlgEquiv.isometry (operatorRelabel d ℓ e)).continuous\n    exact StarSubalgebra.topologicalClosure_minimal h hc hA\n  exact let f := (operatorRelabel d ℓ (Equiv.addLeft x)).toStarAlgHom\n    (f.comp (quasiLocalAlgebra d ℓ).subtype).codRestrict (quasiLocalAlgebra d ℓ)\n      (fun A => relabel_mem d ℓ (Equiv.addLeft x) A.property)"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "magneticLevel",
      "statement_lean": "def magneticLevel (ℓ : ℕ) (k : Level ℓ) : ℝ := (k : ℝ) - (ℓ : ℝ) / 2"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "spinZ",
      "statement_lean": "def spinZ (d ℓ : ℕ) (x : Site d) : QuasiLocal d ℓ :=\n  ∑ k : Level ℓ, (magneticLevel ℓ k : ℂ) • localUnit d ℓ x k k"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "spinPlus",
      "statement_lean": "def spinPlus (d ℓ : ℕ) (x : Site d) : QuasiLocal d ℓ :=\n  ∑ k : Fin ℓ,\n    (Real.sqrt ((ℓ / 2 : ℝ) * (ℓ / 2 + 1) -\n      magneticLevel ℓ k.castSucc * (magneticLevel ℓ k.castSucc + 1)) : ℂ) •\n      localUnit d ℓ x k.succ k.castSucc"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "spinMinus",
      "statement_lean": "def spinMinus (d ℓ : ℕ) (x : Site d) : QuasiLocal d ℓ := star (spinPlus d ℓ x)"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "spinX",
      "statement_lean": "def spinX (d ℓ : ℕ) (x : Site d) : QuasiLocal d ℓ :=\n  (1 / 2 : ℂ) • (spinPlus d ℓ x + spinMinus d ℓ x)"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "spinY",
      "statement_lean": "def spinY (d ℓ : ℕ) (x : Site d) : QuasiLocal d ℓ :=\n  (1 / (2 * Complex.I) : ℂ) • (spinPlus d ℓ x - spinMinus d ℓ x)"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "NearestNeighbor",
      "statement_lean": "def NearestNeighbor {d : ℕ} (x y : Site d) : Prop :=\n  ∑ i : Fin d, |x i - y i| = (1 : ℤ)"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "volumeEdges",
      "statement_lean": "def volumeEdges {d : ℕ} (Λ : Finset (Site d)) : Finset (Site d × Site d) :=\n  (Λ ×ˢ Λ).filter fun p => NearestNeighbor p.1 p.2 ∧ WellOrderingRel p.1 p.2"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "hamiltonian",
      "statement_lean": "def hamiltonian (d ℓ : ℕ) (Λ : Finset (Site d)) : QuasiLocal d ℓ :=\n  -∑ e ∈ volumeEdges Λ,\n    (spinX d ℓ e.1 * spinX d ℓ e.2 + spinY d ℓ e.1 * spinY d ℓ e.2 +\n      spinZ d ℓ e.1 * spinZ d ℓ e.2)"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "box",
      "statement_lean": "def box (d n : ℕ) : Finset (Site d) :=\n  Fintype.piFinset fun _ : Fin d => Finset.Icc (-(n : ℤ)) (n : ℤ)"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "finiteDynamics",
      "statement_lean": "def finiteDynamics (d ℓ : ℕ) (Λ : Finset (Site d)) (t : ℝ)\n    (A : QuasiLocal d ℓ) : QuasiLocal d ℓ :=\n  NormedSpace.exp ((Complex.I * (t : ℂ)) • hamiltonian d ℓ Λ) * A *\n    NormedSpace.exp ((-(Complex.I * (t : ℂ))) • hamiltonian d ℓ Λ)"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "dynamics",
      "statement_lean": "def dynamics (d ℓ : ℕ) (t : ℝ) (A : QuasiLocal d ℓ) : QuasiLocal d ℓ :=\n  Filter.limUnder Filter.atTop (fun n : ℕ => finiteDynamics d ℓ (box d n) t A)"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "DynamicsConverges",
      "statement_lean": "def DynamicsConverges (d ℓ : ℕ) : Prop :=\n  ∀ (t : ℝ) (A : QuasiLocal d ℓ),\n    Filter.Tendsto (fun n : ℕ => finiteDynamics d ℓ (box d n) t A)\n      Filter.atTop (nhds (dynamics d ℓ t A))"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "structure",
      "name": "State",
      "statement_lean": "structure State (d ℓ : ℕ) where\n  functional : QuasiLocal d ℓ →L[ℂ] ℂ\n  normalized : functional 1 = 1\n  positive : ∀ A, (functional (star A * A)).im = 0 ∧\n    0 ≤ (functional (star A * A)).re"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "TranslationInvariant",
      "statement_lean": "def TranslationInvariant {d ℓ : ℕ} (ω : State d ℓ) : Prop :=\n  ∀ (x : Site d) (A : QuasiLocal d ℓ), ω.functional (translation d ℓ x A) = ω.functional A"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "IsKMS",
      "statement_lean": "def IsKMS {d ℓ : ℕ} (β : ℝ) (ω : State d ℓ) : Prop :=\n  0 < β ∧ ∀ A B : QuasiLocal d ℓ, ∃ F : ℂ → ℂ,\n    ContinuousOn F {z | 0 ≤ z.im ∧ z.im ≤ β} ∧\n    DifferentiableOn ℂ F {z | 0 < z.im ∧ z.im < β} ∧\n    (∃ C : ℝ, ∀ z : ℂ, 0 ≤ z.im → z.im ≤ β → ‖F z‖ ≤ C) ∧\n    (∀ t : ℝ, F t = ω.functional (A * dynamics d ℓ t B)) ∧\n    (∀ t : ℝ, F ((t : ℂ) + Complex.I * β) = ω.functional (dynamics d ℓ t B * A))"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "theorem",
      "name": "spontaneous_magnetization",
      "statement_lean": "theorem spontaneous_magnetization (d ℓ : ℕ) (hd : 3≤d) (hℓ : 1≤ℓ) :\n    DynamicsConverges d ℓ ∧\n      ∃ β₀ : ℝ,0<β₀ ∧ ∀ β : ℝ,β₀≤β →\n        ∃ ω : State d ℓ,TranslationInvariant ω ∧ IsKMS β ω ∧\n          (ω.functional (spinZ d ℓ 0)).im=0 ∧\n          (ℓ:ℝ)/8≤(ω.functional (spinZ d ℓ 0)).re := by\n  sorry"
    }
  ],

    "text": "The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):\n\n- [Spontaneous magnetization in the quantum Heisenberg ferromagnet](../../preprints/Spontaneous-magnetization-in-the-quantum-Heisenberg-ferromagnet-September-24-2026/paper.pdf)\n\n## Scope\n\nThe formalization proves spontaneous magnetization for the nearest-neighbor isotropic quantum Heisenberg ferromagnet on $\\mathbb Z^d$ for every $d\\ge3$ and every spin $S\\in\\{\\tfrac12,1,\\tfrac32,\\ldots\\}$. At every sufficiently low positive temperature, it constructs a translation-invariant equilibrium state satisfying the KMS condition for the zero-field dynamics and having magnetization at least $S/4$.\n\nIt also proves convergence of the finite-volume dynamics to the infinite-volume dynamics used in the KMS statement."

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


For every dimension <i>d</i> ≥ 3 and every spin $`S\in\{\frac12,1,\frac32,\ldots\}`$, we prove that the nearest-neighbor isotropic quantum Heisenberg ferromagnet has a translation-invariant, spontaneously magnetized equilibrium state at every sufficiently low positive temperature. The same state satisfies the KMS condition for the zero-field dynamics and has magnetization at least $`S/4`$. This resolves the low-temperature ordering problem in the spontaneous-magnetization formulation.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

The nearest-neighbor quantum Heisenberg ferromagnet assigns a quantum spin to each lattice site and favors parallel neighboring spins. Its Hamiltonian is invariant under simultaneous rotations of all spins. Spontaneous magnetization asks whether, at zero applied field, an infinite-volume equilibrium state can nevertheless select a direction and have a nonzero one-site spin expectation. The distinction between an individual ordered state and its rotation-invariant average is essential.

<a id="sec:model"></a>
#### The model and the result

<a id="eq:hamiltonian"></a>
Fix an integer $d\geq3$ and a spin $S\in\{\frac12,1,\frac32,\ldots\}$. At each site use $\mathbb C^{2S+1}$ with orthonormal basis $(e_m)_{m=-S}^{S}$ and operators $$S^z e_m=m e_m,\qquad
 S^+e_m=\sqrt{S(S+1)-m(m+1)}\,e_{m+1},\qquad S^+e_S=0.$$ Set $S^-=(S^+)^*$, $S^x=(S^++S^-)/2$ and $S^y=(S^+-S^-)/(2\mathrm i)$, and write $S_x^a$ for the corresponding operator at a site $x$. For finite $\Lambda\subset\mathbb Z^d$, the free-boundary Hamiltonian at coupling one is $$\label{eq:hamiltonian}
 H_\Lambda
 =-\sum_{\substack{\{x,y\}\subset\Lambda\\|x-y|_1=1}}
       \sum_{a\in\{x,y,z\}} S_x^aS_y^a.$$ The outer sum counts each unordered edge once; in its inner sum the letters $a=x,y,z$ designate spin components.

For a finite set $F\subset\mathbb Z^d$, let $\mathcal A_F=\bigotimes_{x\in F}M_{2S+1}(\mathbb C)$, with the usual embeddings obtained by adjoining identity factors. The quasi-local algebra $\mathcal A$ is the norm completion of their union. The bounded finite-range interaction in <a href="#eq:hamiltonian" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:hamiltonian">[eq:hamiltonian]</a> defines a dynamics $\tau_t$ by the norm limit, on local observables, of $$\tau_t^\Lambda(A)=e^{\mathrm itH_\Lambda}A e^{-\mathrm itH_\Lambda}$$ along increasing boxes. This limit is independent of the boxes. We use the resulting norm-continuous orbit $t\mapsto\tau_t(A)$ for every $A\in\mathcal A$. The locality estimates underlying this construction originate in Lieb and Robinson (Lieb and Robinson 1972) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[LiebRobinson1972](#ref-LiebRobinson1972)]. Existence and independence of the exhausting boxes follow from (Nachtergaele, Ogata, and Sims 2006, Theorem 2.2) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[NachtergaeleOgataSims2006](#ref-NachtergaeleOgataSims2006)]; its hypotheses are checked when we use this dynamics in Section <a href="#subsec:deep-dynamics" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="subsec:deep-dynamics">8.2</a>.

<a id="eq:kms-definition"></a>
A state is a positive linear functional $\omega$ on $\mathcal A$ with $\omega(1)=1$. For $0<\beta<\infty$, it is a $\beta$-KMS state for $\tau$ if, for every $A,B\in\mathcal A$, there is a bounded function $F_{A,B}$, continuous on the closed strip $0\leq\operatorname{Im}z\leq\beta$ and analytic in its interior, such that $$\label{eq:kms-definition}
 F_{A,B}(t)=\omega(A\tau_t(B)),\qquad
 F_{A,B}(t+\mathrm i\beta)=\omega(\tau_t(B)A)
 \quad(t\in\mathbb R).$$ This is the algebraic equilibrium condition developed by Haag, Hugenholtz and Winnink (Haag, Hugenholtz, and Winnink 1967) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[HaagHugenholtzWinnink1967](#ref-HaagHugenholtzWinnink1967)]. We call such a state an equilibrium state at inverse temperature $\beta$. If $T_x$ denotes translation by $x\in\mathbb Z^d$, translation invariance means $\omega\circ T_x=\omega$ for every $x$.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *For every integer $d\geq3$ and every $S\in\{\frac12,1,\frac32,\ldots\}$, there is a finite $\beta_0(d,S)>0$ such that, for every finite $\beta\geq\beta_0(d,S)$, the dynamics associated with <a href="#eq:hamiltonian" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:hamiltonian">[eq:hamiltonian]</a> has a translation-invariant $\beta$-KMS state $\omega$ satisfying $$\omega(S_0^z)\ge S/4.$$*

</div>

Thus the zero-field model has spontaneously magnetized equilibrium states at every sufficiently low positive temperature, for each allowed spin. This magnetization is a property of the chosen state and need not be retained by a rotation-invariant average.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### Historical context and significance

Heisenberg’s exchange model provided a quantum mechanism for ferromagnetism (Heisenberg 1928) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[Heisenberg1928](#ref-Heisenberg1928)]. Bloch’s spin-wave picture predicted the low-temperature reduction of magnetization, and Dyson developed the theory of interacting spin waves and their thermodynamic corrections (Bloch 1930; Dyson 1956a, 1956b) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[Bloch1930](#ref-Bloch1930), [Dyson1956Interactions](#ref-Dyson1956Interactions), [Dyson1956Thermodynamics](#ref-Dyson1956Thermodynamics)]. These predictions explain why an ordered phase is expected; proving that a zero-field equilibrium state retains a nonzero spin expectation requires control at all length scales at one fixed positive temperature.

The dimension restriction has a rigorous basis. The Mermin–Wagner theorem excludes positive-temperature ferromagnetism in one and two dimensions for finite-range isotropic Heisenberg models (Mermin and Wagner 1966) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[MerminWagner1966](#ref-MerminWagner1966)]. In dimensions at least three, Fröhlich, Simon and Spencer established low-temperature ordering for classical continuous-spin models through infrared bounds (Fröhlich, Simon, and Spencer 1976, Theorem 3.1) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[FrohlichSimonSpencer1976](#ref-FrohlichSimonSpencer1976)]. Dyson, Lieb and Simon proved quantum antiferromagnetic ordering for spin at least one in those dimensions, and for spin one half in sufficiently large dimension (Dyson, Lieb, and Simon 1978, Theorem 6.2) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[DysonLiebSimon1978](#ref-DysonLiebSimon1978)]. Kennedy, Lieb and Shastry subsequently proved ground-state Néel order for the spin-one-half antiferromagnet on the three-dimensional cubic lattice (Kennedy, Lieb, and Shastry 1988) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[KennedyLiebShastry1988](#ref-KennedyLiebShastry1988)]. The corresponding ferromagnetic problem resisted this approach: the reflection-positivity argument does not provide the needed ferromagnetic Duhamel infrared bound, as explained by Dyson, Lieb and Simon (Dyson, Lieb, and Simon 1978, 339–40) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[DysonLiebSimon1978](#ref-DysonLiebSimon1978)].

Probabilistic representations offer another route. Powers related the isotropic Heisenberg model to random walks on the permutation group (Powers 1976) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[Powers1976](#ref-Powers1976)]. Conlon and Solovej developed random-walk representations for the Heisenberg model and proved a low-temperature free-energy upper bound for spin one half (Conlon and Solovej 1991a, 1991b) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[ConlonSolovej1991Representation](#ref-ConlonSolovej1991Representation), [ConlonSolovej1991UpperBound](#ref-ConlonSolovej1991UpperBound)]. Tóth’s exchange-cycle representation sharpened the pressure bound (Tóth 1993) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[Toth1993](#ref-Toth1993)]. General-spin geometric representations were developed by Nachtergaele (Nachtergaele 1994a, 1994b) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[Nachtergaele1994Stochastic](#ref-Nachtergaele1994Stochastic), [Nachtergaele1994Quasistates](#ref-Nachtergaele1994Quasistates)]; the symmetric-slot and endpoint-permutation formulation is also given by Björnberg, Fröhlich and Ueltschi (Björnberg, Fröhlich, and Ueltschi 2020, sec. 3.2) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[BjornbergFrohlichUeltschi2020](#ref-BjornbergFrohlichUeltschi2020)]. Their ordering results concern the complete graph, whereas the representation rests on finite-spin algebra. Section <a href="#sec:loops" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:loops">2</a> rederives that algebra for the nearest-neighbor interaction and its normalization here. Positive loop weights alone do not establish ordering: the needed additional estimate controls how often an interior cycle avoids pins on the boundary.

Two algebraic methods enter this pin comparison. The stable-polynomial and negative-dependence results of Borcea, Brändén and Liggett (Borcea, Brändén, and Liggett 2009) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[BorceaBrandenLiggett2009](#ref-BorceaBrandenLiggett2009)] underlie the simultaneous pin test in Section <a href="#sec:pin-test" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:pin-test">3</a>. The exterior-moment identity used in Section <a href="#sec:exposure" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:exposure">4</a> belongs to the determinant-preserving framework of Dereziński, Liang and Mahoney (Dereziński, Liang, and Mahoney 2020) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[DerezinskiLiangMahoney2020](#ref-DerezinskiLiangMahoney2020)]: expected minors agree with the minors of the mean, and this property is preserved under independent matrix products. The conditional next-pin formula needed here is derived in Section <a href="#sec:exposure" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:exposure">4</a>.

Correggi, Giuliani and Seiringer proved the fixed-spin low-temperature free-energy asymptotics predicted by spin-wave theory in three dimensions (Correggi, Giuliani, and Seiringer 2015, Theorem 2.1) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[CorreggiGiulianiSeiringer2015](#ref-CorreggiGiulianiSeiringer2015)]. Their bounds also control correlations on a temperature-dependent finite length scale of order $\beta^{5/4}$ (Correggi, Giuliani, and Seiringer 2015, equations (2.6)–(2.8)) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[CorreggiGiulianiSeiringer2015](#ref-CorreggiGiulianiSeiringer2015)]. More recently, Klippel proved the corresponding free-energy asymptotics in two dimensions (Klippel 2026) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[Klippel2026FreeEnergy](#ref-Klippel2026FreeEnergy)]. These are thermodynamic and finite-scale advances; the present result constructs an ordered state at a fixed positive temperature in $d\ge3$.

Lieb explicitly posed the quantum ferromagnetic ordering problem in 1999 (Lieb 1999, Problem A) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[Lieb1999](#ref-Lieb1999)]. Seiringer’s 2025 account records it using a volume-averaged finite-volume two-point order parameter (Seiringer 2025) <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[Seiringer2025](#ref-Seiringer2025)]. Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> resolves the low-temperature ordering problem in the spontaneous-magnetization formulation stated here: it constructs a translation-invariant zero-field KMS state selecting a spin direction, for every allowed spin and every $d\ge3$. The precise conclusion is the one-point state assertion above; the finite-volume correlation criterion in the cited account is a different formulation and is not used in this proof.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### Proof strategy and the main mechanisms

The proof starts in finite cubes. Replace a spin $S$ by $\ell=2S$ spin-$\frac12$ slots at its site and project onto their symmetric subspace. Rate-$\frac12$ Poisson transpositions across neighboring sites, together with uniform within-site permutations at the time seam, give an exchange-path representation. Each cycle has weight two, corresponding to its two spin colors. A *pin* is a slot-time point whose cycle is required to have the up color; a cycle meeting a pin has weight one. Section <a href="#sec:loops" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:loops">2</a> derives the full spectral law of magnetization and a bound on inserted exchange marks under these pin laws.

Pin every boundary slot throughout the time period, and choose a finite time grid. We prove, by downward induction on the number of grid pins, that under every such pin law the probability that $m$ prescribed points all belong to cycles missing the pins is at most $p^m$, for a small fixed $p$. The test points lie at one time and form a sparse set: the number in a spatial ball grows at most a small power of its radius. This restriction is enough both to control obstacle components and, at the end, to bound the probability for a single interior slot.

The induction step adds the test set $T$ to the existing pins $E$, forming $P=E\cup T$. The bound for this larger pin set is already available. Section <a href="#sec:pin-test" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:pin-test">3</a> uses stable multiaffine permutation polynomials to show that the desired avoidance probability under the $E$-law is at most $(2r)^m$. Here $r$ is the mean fraction of points of $T$ whose lines, under the $P$-law, return to $T$ at positive elapsed time before reaching $E$. It therefore suffices to use the larger-pin induction hypothesis to prove $r\le p/2$.

For this return estimate, expose exactly the cycles that miss $P$. The unexposed slots support independent heat exchanges and prescribed transports, conditioned on every remaining cycle meeting a pin. Section <a href="#sec:exposure" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:exposure">4</a> first identifies this conditional law and its exposure marginal. An exterior-power and determinant calculation then identifies the conditional next-pin first moment with that of a single Markov walk using fresh randomness on successive periods. The exposure itself is still averaged under its original $P$-law; no independence of the conditioned cycles is required.

Ignore the other interior pins in the walk’s stopping rule, retaining boundary killing. Let $U$ be its one-period kernel, and let $f$ be one on $T$ and elsewhere the probability of a future visit to $T$ before killing. Section <a href="#sec:exposure" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:exposure">4</a> proves $$m(1-r)\ge\mathbb E_P\langle f,(I-U)f\rangle.$$ The needed lower bound on this quadratic loss is nearly $m$: a bound of $m(1-\varepsilon)$ gives $r\le\varepsilon$. Write $f=\mathbf1_T+g$. The two summands are initially disjoint, but their propagated masses can overlap; controlling that overlap is the main analytic task.

The larger-pin induction hypothesis bounds the probability that every site in a prescribed sparse collection has no unexposed slots. Section <a href="#sec:sparse" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:sparse">5</a> turns this information into short surviving connections near most anchors and bounds on the sizes of distant obstacle components. The first property supplies a local Nash inequality, which spreads the mass initially on $T$. The second allows Section <a href="#sec:flows" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:flows">6</a> to route decaying finite-energy flows through the available sites. These flows control the pairing of the spread mass with the propagated $g$ by its Dirichlet energy at that same time.

Section <a href="#sec:leakage" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:leakage">7</a> applies these estimates in short portions at both ends of a period. A weighted time average absorbs the pairing into the energy dissipated by the heat flow, yielding a quadratic loss at least $m(1-\varepsilon(p))$, with $\varepsilon(p)=o(p)$. Choose $p$ small enough that $2\varepsilon(p)\le p$; the pin test then closes the finite downward induction. Its singleton conclusion says that under continuous boundary pinning an interior slot is unlikely to lie on a cycle avoiding the boundary.

Finally, requiring every boundary-touching cycle to be up costs only an exponential in the boundary size. The pin estimate therefore gives a macroscopic magnetization tail in the free zero-field Gibbs state. Section <a href="#sec:kms" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:kms">8</a> selects this tail with a homogeneous field tending to zero as the boxes grow, and averages translations. It verifies both KMS strip boundaries for the same limiting state and the original zero-field dynamics, completing Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>.

<a id="sec:hard-core"></a>
#### A hard-core boson consequence

The spin-one-half correspondence of Matsubara and Matsuda (Matsubara and Matsuda 1956, secs. 2–3) <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[MatsubaraMatsuda1956](#ref-MatsubaraMatsuda1956)] turns the ordered state into a state with averaged off-diagonal long-range order. For $S=\frac12$, put $b_x=S_x^-$ and $n_x=b_x^\dagger b_x=S_x^z+\frac12$. If $E_\Lambda$ is the set of unordered nearest-neighbor edges in $\Lambda$, then <a href="#eq:hamiltonian" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:hamiltonian">[eq:hamiltonian]</a> becomes $$H_\Lambda
 =-\frac12\sum_{\{x,y\}\in E_\Lambda}
       (b_x^\dagger b_y+b_y^\dagger b_x)
  -\sum_{\{x,y\}\in E_\Lambda}
       (n_x-\tfrac12)(n_y-\tfrac12).$$ This is the particle–hole-symmetric attractive hard-core hopping model. For each inverse temperature in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, rotate the full mean-spin vector $(\omega(S_0^x),\omega(S_0^y),\omega(S_0^z))$ to the positive $x$ direction. Simultaneous spin rotation commutes with the zero-field dynamics and translations, so the rotated state $\nu$ is again a translation-invariant $\beta$-KMS state. It satisfies $\nu(b_x)=r\ge1/8$ and $\nu(n_x)=1/2$, where $r$ is the length of the original mean-spin vector. In particular, it breaks the $U(1)$ symmetry $b_x\mapsto e^{-i\theta}b_x$.

For every nonempty finite $\Lambda$, set $B_\Lambda=\sum_{x\in\Lambda}b_x$ and $a_\Lambda=|\Lambda|^{-1/2}B_\Lambda$. Positivity gives $\nu(B_\Lambda^\dagger B_\Lambda)\ge|\nu(B_\Lambda)|^2
\ge|\Lambda|^2/64$, hence $$\frac1{|\Lambda|^2}\sum_{x,y\in\Lambda}\nu(b_x^\dagger b_y)
       \ge\frac1{64},
 \qquad
 \nu(a_\Lambda^\dagger a_\Lambda)\ge\frac{|\Lambda|}{64}.$$ The first bound gives averaged off-diagonal long-range order; the diagonal contribution is only $1/(2|\Lambda|)$. Since $\nu(\sum_{x\in\Lambda}n_x)=|\Lambda|/2$, the uniform-mode occupation is at least $1/32$ of the expected particle number. These bounds concern restrictions of the chosen infinite-volume state, not finite-volume Gibbs states.

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
#### Conventions

Unless a different norm is displayed, spatial distances and balls use the sup norm on $\mathbb Z^d$; lattice edges always join nearest neighbors. All matrix kernels for one-particle motion act on column vectors of masses, so their columns describe a fixed starting point. Inner products and $\ell^p$ norms on slots use counting measure. Unless explicitly indicated otherwise, constants may depend on $d$ and $S$ and on explicitly fixed geometric parameters, but not on the volume, the pin set, or the time period. The order of choosing the small pinning parameter and the geometric scales is specified in Sections <a href="#sec:sparse" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:sparse">5</a> and <a href="#sec:leakage" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:leakage">7</a>.

<a id="sec:loops"></a>
### Spin slots, loops, and pins

We first express the finite-volume Gibbs state by exchange paths. The representation will identify the entire spectral distribution of total magnetization, which is needed when a small field is introduced at the end of the proof. We then define the pin laws used in the intervening probabilistic argument and establish their two elementary measure bounds. Powers related the isotropic Heisenberg model to random walks on permutations (Powers 1976) <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[Powers1976](#ref-Powers1976)]. Tóth gave the random-stirring representation for spin $1/2$ (Tóth 1993) <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[Toth1993](#ref-Toth1993)]. Nachtergaele developed general-spin symmetric-slot representations (Nachtergaele 1994b, sec. 2.2, equations (2.23)–(2.25), and Theorem 2.4) <a id="pm-cite-26"></a>[[Nachtergaele1994Quasistates](#ref-Nachtergaele1994Quasistates)]; see also his account of geometric representations (Nachtergaele 1994a) <a id="pm-cite-27"></a>[[Nachtergaele1994Stochastic](#ref-Nachtergaele1994Stochastic)]. For the endpoint-permutation realization and the magnetization transform, see also Björnberg, Fröhlich and Ueltschi (Björnberg, Fröhlich, and Ueltschi 2020, sec. 3.2, Proposition 3.2 and equation (3.19)) <a id="pm-cite-28"></a>[[BjornbergFrohlichUeltschi2020](#ref-BjornbergFrohlichUeltschi2020)]. Their representation is presented for the complete graph with a different coupling normalization. We derive the finite-graph algebra here for our nearest-neighbor Hamiltonian, including its rates and full spectral law, before introducing the pin weights needed in the proof.

Fix a finite cube $\Lambda\subset\mathbb Z^d$, $S\in\{\tfrac12,1,\tfrac32,\ldots\}$, and $0<\beta<\infty$. Set $\ell=2S$ and introduce the *slot set* $$V_\Lambda=\Lambda\times\{1,\ldots,\ell\}.$$ A slot $i=(x,a)$ is located at the site $x$. Two slots form a *slot edge* if their sites are nearest neighbors; all $\ell^2$ pairs above each lattice edge are included. Let $\mathcal E_\Lambda$ be this set of unordered slot edges and put $q_\Lambda=|\mathcal E_\Lambda|$. On $$\widehat{\mathcal H}_\Lambda
   =\bigotimes_{i\in V_\Lambda}\mathbb C^2$$ write $s_i^a$ for the spin-$\tfrac12$ matrix in direction $a$ acting on slot $i$. For a permutation $\pi$ of the slots, $\mathcal U_\pi$ denotes the tensor permutation sending the factor originally at $i$ to $\pi(i)$; in particular $\mathcal U_\pi\mathcal U_\rho=\mathcal U_{\pi\circ\rho}$.

<a id="stmt-lem-slot-realization"></a>
<div id="lem:slot-realization" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:slot-hamiltonian"></a>
**Lemma 2** (Symmetric-slot realization). *At each site $x$, the operators $\widehat S_x^a=\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}s_{(x,j)}^a$ restrict on $\operatorname{Sym}^{\ell}(\mathbb C^2)$ to the spin-$S$ matrices defined in Section <a href="#sec:model" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:model">1.1</a>. The orthogonal projection onto this subspace is $$\mathcal S_x=\frac1{\ell!}
      \sum_{\kappa\in\mathfrak S_\ell}\mathcal U_{\kappa,x}.$$ With $\mathcal S_\Lambda=\prod_{x\in\Lambda}\mathcal S_x$, define $$\widehat H_\Lambda
 =-\sum_{\{x,y\}\subset\Lambda:\,|x-y|_1=1}
          \sum_{a=x,y,z}\widehat S_x^a\widehat S_y^a,
 \qquad
 \widehat M_\Lambda=\sum_{i\in V_\Lambda}s_i^z.$$ Both operators commute with every within-site permutation and with $\mathcal S_\Lambda$. Their restrictions to $\operatorname{ran}\mathcal S_\Lambda$ are unitarily equivalent to $H_\Lambda$ and $M_\Lambda=\sum_{x\in\Lambda}S_x^z$, respectively. Moreover, $$\label{eq:slot-hamiltonian}
 \widehat H_\Lambda
   =-\frac12\sum_{\{i,j\}\in\mathcal E_\Lambda}
                   \mathcal U_{(ij)}
       +\frac{q_\Lambda}{4}I.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-slot-realization"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-slot-realization</summary>

*Proof.* Let $\psi_k$ be the normalized sum of the $\binom\ell k$ elementary tensor vectors having exactly $k$ up spins, for $0\le k\le\ell$. These form an orthonormal basis of the symmetric subspace, and $$\widehat S^z\psi_k=(k-\ell/2)\psi_k.$$ For $0\le k<\ell$, each elementary vector with $k+1$ up spins occurs $k+1$ times in $\sum_j s_j^+\psi_k$. Consequently $$\widehat S^+\psi_k
  =(k+1)\sqrt{\frac{\binom\ell{k+1}}{\binom\ell k}}\,\psi_{k+1}
  =\sqrt{(\ell-k)(k+1)}\,\psi_{k+1}.$$ Also $\widehat S^+\psi_\ell=0$. Under $m=k-S$, the squared coefficient is $S(S+1)-m(m+1)$. The lowering, $x$, and $y$ matrices follow by adjunction and the definitions. Thus $e_m\mapsto\psi_{S+m}$ gives the claimed unitary identification.

The average over the finite permutation group is self-adjoint and idempotent, and its range is exactly the invariant subspace, proving the projection formula. Conjugation by a within-site permutation merely reorders the summands of each $\widehat S_x^a$. It therefore commutes with $\widehat H_\Lambda$ and $\widehat M_\Lambda$. Equivalently, in the slot-edge expression, conjugation permutes the complete collection of $\ell^2$ slot pairs above every incident lattice edge. This last observation is essential: an individual inter-site transposition need not commute with $\mathcal S_x$.

Finally, on two spin-$\tfrac12$ factors, direct calculation in the up/down basis gives $$\sum_{a=x,y,z}s_i^as_j^a
       =\frac12\mathcal U_{(ij)}-\frac14 I.$$ Summing this identity over the slot edges proves <a href="#eq:slot-hamiltonian" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:slot-hamiltonian">[eq:slot-hamiltonian]</a> and the asserted restrictions. ◻

</details>

The *base exchange law* $\mathbb P_{\mathrm b}$ is defined as follows. Independently on each edge of $\mathcal E_\Lambda$, put a Poisson process of rate $1/2$ on the time interval $[0,\beta)$. At a mark, exchange the two slot lines. At the end of the interval, apply an independent uniform permutation of the $\ell$ slots at each site, and then identify time $\beta$ with time $0$. An *exchange picture* $\omega$ comprises these marks and endpoint permutations. Following its lines forward through repeated copies of the same picture gives directed loops, which we call its *cycles*. They correspond to the cycles of its one-period slot permutation $\pi_\omega$ and can wind through several periods. Let $c(\omega)$ be their number. In particular, $c(\omega)\le |V_\Lambda|$.

Slot-time points belong to $V_\Lambda\times(\mathbb R/\beta\mathbb Z)$. At an exchange we use the slot just after the exchange; time $0$ is understood just after the within-site endpoint permutations and the wrapping of time. Thus every cycle has a well-defined set of slots at the time-zero cut. Write $a_C$ for the number of these slots on a cycle $C$; then $\sum_C a_C=|V_\Lambda|$.

<a id="stmt-prop-spin-loops"></a>
<div id="prop:spin-loops" class="proposition">

<a id="eq:loop-partition-function"></a>
<a id="eq:spectral-loop-law"></a>
**Proposition 3** (Exact spin-to-loop representation). *For every finite cube $\Lambda$, spin $S$, and $0<\beta<\infty$ as above, the partition function is $$\label{eq:loop-partition-function}
 Z_\Lambda(\beta):=\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}_{\mathcal H_\Lambda}e^{-\beta H_\Lambda}
       =e^{\beta q_\Lambda/4}\,
                     \mathbb E_{\mathrm b}2^{c(\omega)}.$$ More generally, for every function $\Phi$ on the finite spectrum of $M_\Lambda$, $$\label{eq:spectral-loop-law}
 \frac{\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}_{\mathcal H_\Lambda}
          \bigl(e^{-\beta H_\Lambda}\Phi(M_\Lambda)\bigr)}
      {Z_\Lambda(\beta)}
 =\frac{\displaystyle\mathbb E_{\mathrm b}
          \sum_{\varepsilon\in\{-1,1\}^{\{\text{cycles of }\omega\}}}
          \Phi\!\left(\frac12\sum_C\varepsilon_C a_C\right)}
        {\displaystyle\mathbb E_{\mathrm b}2^{c(\omega)}}.$$ Thus the spectral law of $M_\Lambda$ is obtained by weighting the base picture by $2^{c(\omega)}$, then coloring each cycle independently up or down with equal probabilities, and summing the corresponding $+1/2$ or $-1/2$ over its slots at time $0$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-spin-loops"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-spin-loops</summary>

<a id="eq:loop-operator-average"></a>
<a id="eq:loop-spectral-trace"></a>
*Proof.* Let $\varphi_t$ be the permutation generated by the inter-site marks up to time $t$, without the endpoint permutations. Independent increments, or the Poisson expansion in time-ordered marks, give $$\mathbb E_{\mathrm b}\mathcal U_{\varphi_\beta}
   =\exp\!\left\{
       \frac\beta2\sum_{\{i,j\}\in\mathcal E_\Lambda}
                    (\mathcal U_{(ij)}-I)\right\}
   =e^{-\beta q_\Lambda/4}e^{-\beta\widehat H_\Lambda}.$$ Indeed, over an interval of length $\,\mathrm dt$, the expectation is $I+\frac12\sum_{\{i,j\}}(\mathcal U_{(ij)}-I)\,\mathrm dt+o(\,\mathrm dt)$; the resulting constant-coefficient matrix differential equation gives the displayed exponential. Averaging the independent endpoint permutations now gives $$\label{eq:loop-operator-average}
 \mathbb E_{\mathrm b}\mathcal U_{\pi_\omega}
    =e^{-\beta q_\Lambda/4}
                   \mathcal S_\Lambda e^{-\beta\widehat H_\Lambda}.$$ Lemma <a href="#lem:slot-realization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:slot-realization">2</a> identifies the trace on the range of $\mathcal S_\Lambda$ with the physical trace. Moreover, $\widehat M_\Lambda$ commutes with every slot permutation, since it is the sum of the same one-slot observable over all slots. Therefore <a href="#eq:loop-operator-average" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:loop-operator-average">[eq:loop-operator-average]</a> yields $$\label{eq:loop-spectral-trace}
 \mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}_{\mathcal H_\Lambda}
       \bigl(e^{-\beta H_\Lambda}\Phi(M_\Lambda)\bigr)
 =e^{\beta q_\Lambda/4}\,
   \mathbb E_{\mathrm b}\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}_{\widehat{\mathcal H}_\Lambda}
       \bigl(\mathcal U_{\pi_\omega}\Phi(\widehat M_\Lambda)\bigr).$$

To calculate the last trace, index the elementary up/down tensor basis by $\eta\in\{-1,1\}^{V_\Lambda}$. The diagonal entry of $\mathcal U_{\pi_\omega}$ at $\eta$ is one exactly when $\eta$ is constant on every cycle of $\pi_\omega$, and is zero otherwise. For such a basis vector the eigenvalue of $\widehat M_\Lambda$ is $\frac12\sum_C\varepsilon_Ca_C$. Hence $$\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}_{\widehat{\mathcal H}_\Lambda}
       \bigl(\mathcal U_{\pi_\omega}\Phi(\widehat M_\Lambda)\bigr)
   =\sum_{\varepsilon\in\{-1,1\}^{\{\text{cycles of }\omega\}}}
          \Phi\!\left(\frac12\sum_C\varepsilon_Ca_C\right).$$ Taking $\Phi=1$ proves <a href="#eq:loop-partition-function" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:loop-partition-function">[eq:loop-partition-function]</a>; dividing <a href="#eq:loop-spectral-trace" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:loop-spectral-trace">[eq:loop-spectral-trace]</a> by it proves <a href="#eq:spectral-loop-law" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:spectral-loop-law">[eq:spectral-loop-law]</a>. In particular, taking $\Phi$ to be the indicator of any spectral subset proves the claim about the spectral distribution, not only its moments. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
#### Pin laws and the boundary limit

The factor two in the representation records the two colors of each cycle. A pin fixes the color of every cycle it meets. This motivates a family of measures in which only the cycles avoiding the pins retain their factor two.

For a deterministic pin set $D\subset V_\Lambda\times(\mathbb R/\beta\mathbb Z)$, let $$n_D(\omega)=\#\{C:C\text{ is a cycle of }\omega,\ C\cap D=\varnothing\},
 \qquad w_D(\omega)=2^{n_D(\omega)},
 \qquad Z_D=\mathbb E_{\mathrm b}w_D.$$ We use finite pin sets and their unions with the continuous boundary set defined below. Their cycle-incidence events are measurable. The *pin law* and its expectation are <a id="eq:pin-law"></a>$$\label{eq:pin-law}
 \mathbb P_D(\,\mathrm d\omega)
      =\frac{w_D(\omega)}{Z_D}\mathbb P_{\mathrm b}(\,\mathrm d\omega),
 \qquad \mathbb E_D[\cdot].$$ The set of slot-time points on cycles missing $D$ is denoted by $$\mathcal B_D(\omega)=\bigcup_{C:\,C\cap D=\varnothing}C.$$ In fixed volume, $1\le w_D\le 2^{|V_\Lambda|}$, so $Z_D$ is finite and strictly positive. The empty-pin law $\mathbb P_\varnothing$ is exactly the picture marginal in Proposition <a href="#prop:spin-loops" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:spin-loops">3</a>.

The *inner boundary* and the continuous boundary pin set are <a id="eq:continuous-boundary-pins"></a>$$\label{eq:continuous-boundary-pins}
 \partial\Lambda
    =\{x\in\Lambda:\exists y\notin\Lambda,\ |x-y|_1=1\},
 \qquad
 D_0=\partial\Lambda\times\{1,\ldots,\ell\}
                       \times(\mathbb R/\beta\mathbb Z).$$ Every slot above a boundary site is therefore pinned at every time. The following fixed-volume approximation allows finite-layer arguments to be applied with this boundary condition.

<a id="stmt-lem-dense-pins"></a>
<div id="lem:dense-pins" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:dense-pin-expectations"></a>
**Lemma 4** (Dense boundary pins). *Fix a finite cube $\Lambda$, $0<\beta<\infty$, and a finite deterministic pin set $F$. Let $(\mathcal C_n)_{n\ge1}$ be increasing finite sets of times, each containing $0$, whose union is dense in $\mathbb R/\beta\mathbb Z$, and put $$D_{0,n}=\partial\Lambda\times\{1,\ldots,\ell\}\times\mathcal C_n,
 \qquad D_n=F\cup D_{0,n},\qquad D=F\cup D_0.$$ For $\mathbb P_{\mathrm b}$-almost every picture there is a finite $n_*(\omega)$ such that, for all $n\ge n_*(\omega)$, $$w_{D_n}(\omega)=w_D(\omega),\qquad
 \mathcal B_{D_n}(\omega)=\mathcal B_D(\omega).$$ The laws $\mathbb P_{D_n}$ converge to $\mathbb P_D$ in total variation. More generally, if $\Phi_n\to\Phi$ almost surely under $\mathbb P_{\mathrm b}$ and $\sup_n|\Phi_n|\le A<\infty$, then $$\label{eq:dense-pin-expectations}
             \mathbb E_{D_n}\Phi_n\longrightarrow\mathbb E_D\Phi.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-dense-pins"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-dense-pins</summary>

*Proof.* In one period the number of Poisson marks is finite almost surely. Their times are distinct and avoid the countable collection of deterministic cut times and pin times, almost surely. A line that visits a boundary site occupies that site on a time interval of positive length: inter-site marks do not coincide, and endpoint permutations preserve the site. This remains true for a visit crossing the time seam. A dense collection of cuts detects every such visit, with every slot included at each boundary cut.

There are only finitely many cycles. For a fixed picture, choose one detecting cut for each cycle that meets $D_0$ and take the largest of their finite indices. After this index every cycle has the same incidence with $D_n$ as with $D$. This proves both eventual equalities. Also $w_{D_n}\downarrow w_D$ and $w_{D_n}\le 2^{|V_\Lambda|}$. Dominated convergence gives $Z_{D_n}\to Z_D$ and $$\mathbb E_{\mathrm b}
       \left|\frac{w_{D_n}}{Z_{D_n}}-\frac{w_D}{Z_D}\right|
       \longrightarrow0,$$ which proves total-variation convergence. Applying dominated convergence to $w_{D_n}\Phi_n$ proves <a href="#eq:dense-pin-expectations" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:dense-pin-expectations">[eq:dense-pin-expectations]</a>. The dominating constant here depends on the fixed volume; no uniform-in-volume bound on the total loop weight is asserted. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-10"></a>
#### Factorial domination for exchange marks

The pin weights alter the distribution of the Poisson marks. The bound needed later is on ordered lists of distinct marks; it does not require independence under a pin law.

Let $X_\Lambda=\mathcal E_\Lambda\times[0,\beta)$ and let $\mu$ be the measure satisfying $\mu(\{e\}\times\,\mathrm dt)=\tfrac12\,\mathrm dt$. Write $\omega_{\mathrm m}$ for the counting measure of inter-site marks in the picture. For $n\ge1$, the notation $(\omega_{\mathrm m})^n_{\ne}$ means ordered $n$-tuples of distinct marks, even when some of their edge labels agree.

<a id="stmt-lem-factorial"></a>
<div id="lem:factorial" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:factorial-domination"></a>
**Lemma 5** (Factorial-measure domination). *For every finite pin set $D$, or such a set united with $D_0$, every integer $n\ge1$, and every nonnegative measurable function $F:X_\Lambda^n\to[0,\infty]$, $$\label{eq:factorial-domination}
 \mathbb E_D\sum_{(u_1,\ldots,u_n)\in(\omega_{\mathrm m})^n_{\ne}}
                 F(u_1,\ldots,u_n)
 \le 2^n\int_{X_\Lambda^n}F(u_1,\ldots,u_n)
                          \prod_{j=1}^n\mu(\,\mathrm du_j).$$ In particular, the estimate holds with the marks restricted to any specified time interval and with any specified chronological order.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-factorial"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-factorial</summary>

<a id="eq:pin-insertion-ratio"></a>
*Proof.* Inserting one inter-site transposition into a picture cuts two directed strands and reconnects them with their outgoing portions exchanged. If they lie on different cycles, those cycles join; if they lie on one cycle, that cycle splits into two. The union of their slot-time strands, and hence all pin incidences on that union, is retained. On joining, the number of cycles missing $D$ cannot increase. On splitting, it increases by at most one: a cycle missing $D$ gives two such cycles, while a cycle meeting $D$ has at least one descendant still meeting $D$. Thus, away from coincident mark times, $$\label{eq:pin-insertion-ratio}
 w_D\!\left(\omega+\sum_{j=1}^n\delta_{u_j}\right)
       \le 2^n w_D(\omega).$$ For finite pins coincidences with pin times have product-intensity measure zero; including $D_0$ does not change the surgery argument. The endpoint permutations are held fixed throughout this insertion.

The identity used to integrate <a href="#eq:pin-insertion-ratio" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:pin-insertion-ratio">[eq:pin-insertion-ratio]</a> is the iterated Mecke formula (Mecke 1967) <a id="pm-cite-29"></a>[[Mecke1967](#ref-Mecke1967)]; its factorial form is stated in (Last and Penrose 2017, Theorem 4.4) <a id="pm-cite-30"></a>[[LastPenrose2017](#ref-LastPenrose2017)]. We derive it here under the base Poisson law, with the pin weight retained inside the integrand. Write $\lambda=\mu(X_\Lambda)$. For a fixed endpoint permutation, expand the Poisson measure as $$e^{-\lambda}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac1{k!}
                      \prod_{j=1}^k\mu(\,\mathrm du_j).$$ In the weighted sum over ordered distinct $n$-tuples, the $k$-mark term has $k!/(k-n)!$ choices of the distinguished marks. Relabel them $u_1,\ldots,u_n$ and sum the remaining $k-n$ marks. Averaging also over the independent endpoint permutations gives the exact identity $$\begin{aligned}
 &\mathbb E_{\mathrm b}\left[
       w_D(\omega)
       \sum_{(u_1,\ldots,u_n)\in(\omega_{\mathrm m})^n_{\ne}}
                    F(u_1,\ldots,u_n)\right]\\
 &\hspace{8mm}=
   \int_{X_\Lambda^n}F(u_1,\ldots,u_n)
       \mathbb E_{\mathrm b}\!\left[
          w_D\!\left(\omega+\sum_{j=1}^n\delta_{u_j}\right)\right]
             \prod_{j=1}^n\mu(\,\mathrm du_j).
\end{aligned}$$ All terms are nonnegative, so this manipulation is justified also when an integral is infinite. By <a href="#eq:pin-insertion-ratio" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:pin-insertion-ratio">[eq:pin-insertion-ratio]</a>, the expectation inside the integral is at most $2^n Z_D$. Division by $Z_D$ proves <a href="#eq:factorial-domination" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:factorial-domination">[eq:factorial-domination]</a>. ◻

</details>

The estimate is unconditional under $\mathbb P_D$. If an independent fresh Poisson family is also sampled, mixed lists of distinct actual and fresh marks satisfy the corresponding product bound: an actual mark contributes $2\mu$, and a fresh mark contributes $\mu$. Indeed, fix which entries in the list come from each family, use independence to factor the two factorial measures, and apply Lemma <a href="#lem:factorial" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:factorial">5</a> to the actual-mark factor. This observation will permit chronological path estimates after averaging over an exchange picture and fresh randomness; it makes no conditional factorial-domination assertion at a fixed path exposure.

We now have the exact spectral representation and a family of pin laws with controlled mark counts. The next section estimates the probability that many prescribed slot-time points simultaneously belong to cycles avoiding the existing pins.

<a id="sec:pin-test"></a>
### A simultaneous pin test

We relate the probability that many specified points lie on cycles missing the pins to a mean return probability after those points are pinned. The estimate uses the complete pin law, before any trajectories are exposed.

<a id="eq:pin-return-fraction"></a>
Fix a finite volume and a finite period $\beta>0$. Let $E$ be a finite pin set, or the union of a finite pin set with $D_0$, and let $T$ be a nonempty finite set of slot points at one time, disjoint from $E$. Put $m=|T|$ and $P=E\cup T$. For $i\in T$, let $\mathcal R_i(E,T)$ be the event that the line started at $i$ reaches $T$ again, at strictly positive elapsed time, before it reaches $E$. The same sampled exchange picture is repeated when following this line through successive periods. Define $$\label{eq:pin-return-fraction}
 r=r(E,T):=\frac1m\sum_{i\in T}
                  \mathbb P_P\bigl(\mathcal R_i(E,T)\bigr).$$ Thus $0\le r\le1$. The positive elapsed-time convention excludes the starting point from the return test.

<a id="stmt-prop-pin-test"></a>
<div id="prop:pin-test" class="proposition">

<a id="eq:simultaneous-pin-test"></a>
